# 1716 Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra Theotokarion / Eight-Tone Marian Canons (Богородичник / Канони Богородиці Осмогласні, Київ: Друкарня Лаври, 1716) <a id="1716-kyiv-pechersk-lavra-theotokarion-eight-tone-marian-canons"></a>

[p1LA] *(Flyleaf — Archival binding sheet)* [p1LA] [Front pastedown / unnumbered archival flyleaf with 18th-century manuscript notations. At top is an early cursive Cyrillic inscription: "From the books of Elijah the Deacon..." In the middle are archival ink shelfmarks: "No. 4n.", "Year 1795, No: 3." At the bottom is a cursive inscription: "Of the Metropolitan..."][^1]

[p1RA] [Title Page within an architectural woodcut border featuring medallions of the Holy Dormition, Saint Prince Vladimir, Saint Andrew the First-Called, the Venerable Anthony and Theodosius of the Caves, Holy Martyrs Boris and Gleb, and a panoramic cityscape of the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra with the engraver's monogram and date 1707:][^2]

*Canons to the Most Holy Theotokos in the Eight Tones,*

appointed to be read at Compline for every day according to the Church Typikon.

Written by Saint John of Damascus.

Printed in types in the Holy Great Miraculous Lavra of the Caves in Kyiv.

In the year from the Nativity of Christ: 1716.

[p2LA] [Full-page woodcut engraving: The Dormition of the Most Holy Theotokos. At the top is inscribed: "The Dormition of the Most Holy Theotokos." The All-Pure Virgin rests upon the bier; behind the bier the Savior in glory holds her all-pure soul in the likeness of an infant in swaddling bands; around stand the Holy Apostles and Angels; in the foreground stands a candlestick with a burning candle; at lower right is the engraver's signature: "MARKO," and at left: "In the year 17...". In the lower cartouche beneath the engraving is inscribed the Troparion of the Dormition:][^3]

*Troparion:* In giving birth thou didst preserve virginity, and in thy dormition thou didst not forsake the world, O Theotokos; thou hast passed over unto life, being the Mother of Life, and by thy supplications thou deliverest our souls from death.

[p2RA] [Woodcut headpiece with foliate acanthus scrolls.]

## PREFACE

"Let us magnify the Mother of God with songs," proclaims the Holy Church unto the people gathered together into the divine temple, who desire every day to magnify the Mother of God, who is blessed by all generations. Yet being unable worthily to praise the true Mother of God, as is truly meet, she blesses her with these her very own songs of praise, saying: "My soul doth magnify the Lord, and my spirit hath rejoiced in God my Savior." In no wise can the rules of ordinary rhetoric encompass her who surpassed all natural laws: "The ordinances of nature are overcome in thee, O pure Virgin." What word can eloquently utter, or what created mind can comprehend, that which is beyond the mind: "Thy childbearing is full of marvels, O All-Pure Bride, Blessed Mother!" What tongue can dare to speak concerning the Mother of God, when even supercelestial luminaries behold her in amazement, being as it were speechless fish? What hand of a scribe can depict with artistic strokes that which is not of nature, but the work of God's good pleasure? Truly all the mysteries of the Theotokos are beyond the mind, all are most glorious. Holy Elizabeth prophesied somewhat in praise, yet being filled with the Holy Spirit she said: "Blessed art thou among women, and blessed is the fruit of thy womb." Another woman also from the crowd cried aloud, saying: "Blessed is the womb that bore Thee, and the breasts which Thou didst suck." Yet she was enlightened by the divine words of Christ preaching the Gospel. The divine prophets also foretold beforehand many feast-day things [p3LA] with types did the divine prophets foretell, not by their own exposition, nor by their own will, but the holy men of God spoke from the Holy Spirit. Yet this small portion of praise, even though introduced from divine revelations, what is it compared to the perfect praise of the Mother of God? It is a drop of water of the same nature as the sea, but small compared to the multitude of the rivers of the sea. It is a star having one radiance with the sun, yet incomparable to the majesty of the sun's brilliance. In order perfectly to magnify the Mother of God, whose majesty the Mighty One hath wrought, scarcely can one find from her very lips anything more acceptable in praise besides her own words, to think upon the dignity of which is the work of the heavenly mind itself. Yet according to the fittingness of love to praise her, even if it is awe-inspiring, nevertheless it is an acceptable deed, as the Church chants: "It were fitting for us through fear to choose silence as being without peril; yet out of love, O Virgin, to weave hymns is a precious work." Great is the work of love, through which are all things, and without which is nothing. To speak with human and angelic tongues is exceedingly glorious, but without love it is a sounding cymbal; to prophesy things to come, to understand secret things, to work miracles—these are something divine, yet without love the worker thereof is as nothing. Love is patient and kind; love does not envy, does not boast, is not proud, does not behave rudely, does not seek its own, is not provoked, loves all things, believes all things, hopes all things. Bearing witness in these manners, unto whom can love fail to be pleasing? Whom does it not properly praise? Magnificent is that which is done by illuminating reason, yet only in part, for we know in part; but in love, what is imperfect? Love is the fulfilling of the Law. Knowledge puffs up, but love builds up. Prophecies shall fail, tongues shall cease, knowledge shall pass away, but love never falls away. Following this most excellent way of love shown forth by the Apostle [p3RA] the God-loving man Saint John of Damascus, having found it, went beyond the words of praise of many saints, and especially of the Mother of God; which canons in the eight tones, usually chanted at Compline, now printed in types, the Holy Church sends forth into the world, if not with voice yet in deed crying out to all the Orthodox, saying: "Let us magnify the Mother of God with songs." For the majesty of her divine childbearing, which magnified our race, blessing the Mother of God, let us chant these supplicatory hymns to her who is our continual intercessor with God. We pray to the Mother of Light, we who after daily labors desire to rest in nocturnal sleep, that she may preserve us from the dark sleep of sin, and from every defiling carnal passion; we prayerfully greet the Star of Jacob, who reveals herself not far from us, but carries upon her hands the Sun of Righteousness, Christ the Lord. That falling asleep in natural sleep, we may not sleep the sleep of death, but in her light we may see again the light of day. And if anyone should walk in the midst of the shadow of death, he shall not fear evil, when he has this Blessed One among women, clothed with the Sun, as his companion. For as here with this Mother he was a son of faith and grace, so also in heaven with her may he be counted worthy to be a son of the day and of light. Which, by the goodness of Christ the true Light, through the intercession of the Virgin Mother of God, and of all the saints who have shone forth in a God-pleasing life, may it be granted unto all of us to obtain.

Prayerfully wishing this,

Archimandrite Ioannikii[^4]

with the whole Brotherhood

of the Holy Great Miraculous Lavra of the Caves in Kyiv.

[p4LA] (*Full-page woodcut engraving: Christ Pantocrator. Above, the Holy Spirit appears as a dove in radiance (*ДХЪ СТЫЙ*). The Savior stands under an archway, blessing with His right hand and holding an open Gospel book inscribed with Matthew 11:28–29; His nimbus bears the cruciform inscription *Ὁ ὪΝ*. Beneath the image is a cartouche with the inscription:*)[^5]

"I am the door: by Me if any man enter in, he shall be saved" (John 10:9).

[Below the cartouche is depicted an Imperial double-headed eagle surrounded by Baroque acanthus foliage.]

[f. 1rA] [Ornamental woodcut headpiece: Medallion of Christ the Savior surrounded by foliate acanthus scrolls.]

*The Beginning of Small Compline.*

*When the priest has given the blessing,*

*We say according to the custom as follows:*

Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.

O Heavenly King, Comforter, the Spirit of Truth, Who art everywhere and fillest all things, Treasury of blessings, and Giver of life: come and abide in us, [f. 1vA] and cleanse us from every stain, and save our souls, O Good One.

Holy God, Holy Mighty, Holy Immortal, have mercy on us. *(Thrice)*

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

O All-Holy Trinity, have mercy on us; Lord, cleanse our sins; Master, pardon our iniquities; Holy One, visit and heal our infirmities for Thy name's sake.

Lord, have mercy. *(Thrice)* Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

Our Father, Who art in the heavens, hallowed be Thy name. Thy kingdom come. Thy will be done, on earth as it is in heaven. Give us this day our daily bread, [f. 2rA] and forgive us our debts, as we forgive our debtors. And lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from the evil one.

Lord, have mercy. *(12 times)* Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

Come, let us worship God our King.

Come, let us worship and fall down before Christ, our King and our God.

Come, let us worship and fall down before Christ Himself, our King and our God.

*Then Psalm 50:*

Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy; and according to the multitude of Thy compassions blot out my transgression.

[f. 2vA] Wash me thoroughly from mine iniquity, and cleanse me from my sin.

For I know mine iniquity, and my sin is continually before me.

Against Thee only have I sinned, and done evil before Thee; that Thou mightest be justified in Thy words, and overcome when Thou art judged.

For behold, I was conceived in iniquities, and in sins did my mother bear me.

For behold, Thou hast loved truth; the hidden and secret things of Thy wisdom hast Thou revealed unto me.

Thou shalt sprinkle me with hyssop, and I shall be cleansed; Thou shalt wash me, and I shall be made whiter than snow.

Thou shalt make me to hear joy and gladness; [f. 3rA] the bones which have been humbled shall rejoice.

Turn Thy face away from my sins, and blot out all mine iniquities.

Create in me a clean heart, O God, and renew a right spirit within me.

Cast me not away from Thy presence, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me.

Restore unto me the joy of Thy salvation, and uphold me with Thy guiding Spirit.

I will teach transgressors Thy ways, and the ungodly shall turn unto Thee.

Deliver me from bloodguiltiness, O God, Thou God of my salvation; and my tongue shall rejoice in Thy righteousness.

O Lord, Thou shalt open my lips, and my mouth shall declare Thy praise.

[f. 3vA] For if Thou hadst desired sacrifice, I would have given it; with whole-burnt offerings Thou wilt not be pleased.

A sacrifice unto God is a broken spirit; a heart that is broken and humbled God will not despise.

Do good, O Lord, in Thy good pleasure unto Zion, and let the walls of Jerusalem be built.

Then shalt Thou be pleased with a sacrifice of righteousness, with oblation and whole-burnt offerings; then shall they offer bullocks upon Thine altar.

*Psalm 69:*

O God, attend unto my help; O Lord, hasten to help me.

Let them be ashamed and confounded that seek after my soul.

Let them be turned back and brought to shame that wish me evils.

[f. 4rA] Let them be turned back immediately in shame that say unto me: Well done, well done!

Let all those that seek Thee, O God, be glad and rejoice in Thee; and let them that love Thy salvation say continually: Let the Lord be magnified!

As for me, I am poor and needy; O God, help me.

Thou art my Helper and my Deliverer; O Lord, tarry not.

*Psalm 142:*

O Lord, hear my prayer; give ear unto my supplication in Thy truth; hear me in Thy righteousness.

And enter not into judgment with Thy servant, for in Thy sight shall no man living be justified.

For the enemy hath persecuted my soul; he hath humbled [f. 4vA] my life down to the earth.

He hath made me to dwell in dark places, as those that have been long dead; and my spirit was despondent within me, my heart within me was troubled.

I remembered the days of old, I meditated on all Thy works, I pondered on the creations of Thy hands.

I stretched forth my hands unto Thee; my soul is unto Thee as a waterless land.

Hear me speedily, O Lord; my spirit hath failed.

Turn not Thy face away from me, lest I become like them that go down into the pit.

Cause me to hear Thy mercy in the morning, for in Thee have I hoped.

Cause me to know, O Lord, the way wherein I should walk, for unto Thee have I lifted up my soul.

Deliver me from mine enemies, O Lord, unto Thee have I fled for refuge; teach me to do Thy will, [f. 5rA] for Thou art my God.

Thy good Spirit shall lead me in the land of uprightness.

For Thy name's sake, O Lord, Thou shalt quicken me; in Thy righteousness Thou shalt bring my soul out of affliction.

And in Thy mercy Thou shalt destroy mine enemies, and perish all them that afflict my soul; for I am Thy servant.

*The Little Doxology:*

Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, good will among men.

We praise Thee, we bless Thee, we worship Thee, we glorify Thee, we give thanks to Thee for Thy great glory.

O Lord, Heavenly King, God the Father Almighty, and O Lord, the Only-Begotten Son, Jesus Christ, and the Holy Spirit; O Lord God, Lamb of God, Son of the Father, Who takest away the sins

[Manuscript inscription at foot: "Belongs to the library of the Trinity-St. Sergius Lavra. In the year 1859."][^6]

[f. 5vA] of the world, have mercy on us. Thou Who takest away the sins of the world, receive our prayer. Thou Who sittest at the right hand of the Father, have mercy on us: for Thou alone art holy, Thou alone art the Lord, Jesus Christ, to the glory of God the Father. Amen.

Every day will I bless Thee, and I will praise Thy name forever, and unto ages of ages.

Lord, Thou hast been our refuge from generation to generation. I said: Lord, have mercy on me, heal my soul, for I have sinned against Thee. Lord, unto Thee have I fled for refuge; teach me to do Thy will, for Thou art my God; for with Thee is the fountain of life: in Thy light shall we see light. Continue Thy mercy unto them that know Thee.

Vouchsafe, O Lord, this night to keep us without sin. Blessed art Thou, O Lord, [f. 6rA] God of our fathers, and praised and glorified is Thy name unto ages. Amen.

Let Thy mercy, O Lord, be upon us, according as we have hoped in Thee. Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach us Thy statutes. Blessed art Thou, O Master, give me understanding in Thy statutes. Blessed art Thou, O Holy One, enlighten us with Thy statutes.

Thy mercy, O Lord, is forever; despise not the works of Thy hands. To Thee praise is due, to Thee hymnody is due, to Thee glory is due, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*And immediately, the Confession of the Orthodox Faith of the First Ecumenical Council of Nicaea and the Second of Constantinople:*

[f. 6vA] *The Symbol of Faith:*

I believe in one God, the Father Almighty, Maker of heaven and earth, and of all things visible and invisible.

And in one Lord Jesus Christ, the Son of God, the Only-Begotten, begotten of the Father before all ages, Light of Light, true God of true God, begotten, not made, of one essence with the Father, by Whom all things were made;

Who for us men and for our salvation came down from heaven, and was incarnate of the Holy Spirit and the Virgin Mary, and became man;

And was crucified also for us under Pontius Pilate, and suffered and was buried;

And rose again on the third day according to the Scriptures; [f. 7rA] And ascended into heaven, and sits at the right hand of the Father;

And shall come again with glory to judge the living and the dead, Whose kingdom shall have no end.

And in the Holy Spirit, the Lord, the Giver of life, Who proceeds from the Father, Who with the Father and the Son together is worshipped and glorified, Who spoke by the prophets.

In one holy, catholic, and apostolic Church.

I confess one baptism for the remission of sins.

I look for the resurrection of the dead;

And the life of the age to come. Amen.

*Then the Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos of the current Tone.*

[f. 7vA] *(Full-page woodcut engraving of the Mother of God holding the Christ Child within an oval medallion, surrounded by twelve roundels depicting Old Testament prefigurations: the Temple of Solomon, the Ark of the Covenant, the Tabernacle, the Seven-branched Menorah, the Table of Showbread, the Tower of David and Closed Gate, the Tablets of the Law, the Golden Pot of Manna, Aaron's Budding Rod, Jacob's Ladder, and the Burning Bush.)*

The intangible God is beheld in the arms of the Virgin;[^7]

Hasten unto Him, all ye mortals!

For the All-Good calls all: "Come,

I am the summit of desire; love Me!"

# TONE 1

## ON SATURDAY AT SMALL COMPLINE (TONE 1)

[f. 1rA] *On Saturday at Small Compline,*[^8]

> **Priest:** "Blessed is our God..."

*Heavenly King... Trisagion, and after Our Father... Lord, have mercy (12 times). Glory... Both now... Come, let us worship (3 times). Psalm 50: Have mercy on me, O God... Psalm 69: O God, attend unto my help... Psalm 142: O Lord, hear my prayer... After this: Glory to God in the highest... Vouchsafe, O Lord, this night... Then: I believe in one God...*

*After this, we chant the Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*Ode 1, Tone 1. Heirmos:*

Delivered from bitter bondage, Israel passed through the impassable as upon dry land; beholding the enemy drowned, in gladness he sings a hymn unto God Who works wonders with a high arm, for He has been glorified.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

The angelic hierarchies were struck with awe in fear, O Queen of all, praising thee; and the great intelligences sing praise because of thy grace, as to the Mother of the Creator; for having given birth to Christ, Thou hast surpassed every order of praise.

*Troparion:*

Tormented by cruel temptations, and troubled by enemies, wretch that I am, I cry out with weeping: Give ear, [f. 1vA] stretch forth thy most rich hand unto me, delivering me; and vouchsafe me to live without danger through thy prayers.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Heal the secret transgressions of my soul with the medicine of compassion, and calm the surging of the flesh, O Theotokos; and thrust the spears and arrows of the enemies firmly into their own hearts.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

The virginal womb, having given birth to Christ, destroyed the ancient man-slaying pasture. Therefore all creation rejoices with me, O Most Pure, seeing that it is brought to life; and with one accord it hymns thy Son and God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Let not the mortal boast in his own wisdom, nor in his wealth; but in the faith of the Lord, crying out with Orthodox faith unto Christ God, and singing continually: Establish me, O Master, upon the rock of Thy commandments.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Jacob the great, once sleeping on the way, marveled as he beheld angels descending from above upon the earth by a ladder, O Virgin; and awaking, he prefigured thee as the heavenly Gate.

*Troparion:*

Cast down into trials by a heavy weight, wretch that I am, and tormented by perilous afflictions: Woe is me, I cry out! O thou who didst bear God, and didst exalt our horn, save me by thy prayers.

*Glory...*

[f. 2rA] *Troparion:*

Having stretched forth Thy mighty hand from heaven, O King of all, Christ my Jesus! Lay beneath the feet of those who faithfully proclaim the Theotokos as Thy Mother the heads of enemies visible and invisible.[^9]

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Isaiah of old, purified by the coal of the Spirit, openly cried out that a Son should be born of thy womb, O most rich Virgin; Whom in these latter times thou hast borne without man for my sake.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Habakkuk of old heard Thy marvelous report, O Christ, and in fear cried out: God shall come from the south, and the Holy One from the dense, overshadowing mountain, to save His anointed ones: glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

As one beautiful and all-honorable among women, rising like an intertwined bond out of the wilderness, and bearing on her arms Christ the Branch, O Mother of God, the leader of the world prefigured thee, crying out: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*

Incline thine ear unto me, O Good One, and behold mine affliction, and the multiplying of my troubles; for stretching forth the eye of my soul unto thee, O Sovereign Lady, and bowing my knees with weeping, I now pray, crying out: Still for me the turmoil of temptations.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Knowing thee to be an invincible rampart, drawn by thy mercy, I thy servant now flee unto thee:

[f. 2vA] and I reckon the arrows of the enemy to be ineffectual like those of infants, O most rich Lady! Therefore, rejoicing, I cry out: Glory to thy childbirth, O Mother of God!

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

The power of the Highest overshadowed thee, O Virgin, by the descent of the Divine Spirit; and then, beyond nature, the Lord of all, having quickened flesh and soul, united them unto Himself, remaining in the same nature.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Shine forth Thy never-setting light, O Christ, in the hearts of those who faithfully hymn Thee; grant us Thy peace, which surpasses understanding: that running from the night of ignorance unto the day of Thy commandments, we may glorify Thee, O Lover of mankind.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Daniel of old, foreseeing thee as a divine unhewn mountain, O All-Hymned, cried out clearly: A stone was cut from thee without hands, O Theotokos, Christ the Savior of the world; Whom the faithful now honor, praising thee, O Bride of God.

*Troparion:*

Falling into many temptations, wretch that I am, praying with anguish and weeping of heart, shamelessly, I thy servant cry out: Deliver me, O Birthgiver of God, from the troubles that encompass my lowliness, and fill me with gladness.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Calm the fierce tempest of my passions by thy mighty prayer, O Good One, who didst bear Christ apart from [f. 3rA] passions; that living now in quietness of soul for the remainder of my life, I may hymn thee in songs.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Tell us, how dost thou carry God in thine arms? And how dost thou suckle Him Who holds all things in His hand, O all-blessed Virgin? "I remain pure" (she said) "even after childbirth, having given birth to Christ God, taking away the debt of Adam and the ancestral mother."

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

I am held fast by measureless passions, and sin is cast upon me like a great whale; but bring me up from corruption, O Christ God, as of old Thou didst Jonah, and by Thy holiness grant me dispassion, that with the voice of praise I may sacrifice unto Thee in a true spirit.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Not parting from the bosom of the Father, the Son before all ages was in the maternal bosom; and in these latter times, He Who is before all ages with the Father came forth from the womb of the Virgin, leading all unto immortal life by ineffable grace.

*Troparion:*

Bound with heavy chains by malice, cast down into the pit of hell, alas for me, I am fallen! Stand before me, appearing from heaven, O pure Maid of God, raising me up by thy prayers, I who am thy servant; and give a hand of help unto him who sings [f. 3vA] thy divine childbirth.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Cast into the pit of destruction, wretch that I am, I have fallen, and many beasts surround me; but, O Sovereign Lady, by thy prayers turn the stones away, and preserve thy servant unharmed; for thou didst bear in thy womb Christ the cornerstone.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Of old the choir of divine prophets proclaimed the images of thy childbirth, O Virgin: calling thee a radiant cloud, a lampstand, a jar of manna, a table, a heavenly mountain, a chamber, an ark, a door, a throne, a palace, a rod, and a paradise, as having given birth to Christ.

*Lord, have mercy (3 times). Glory... Both now... Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*

We all know thee as the Mother of God, who truly appeared as a virgin even after childbirth, fleeing with love unto thy goodness; for we sinners have thee as a protectress, and in temptations we have obtained salvation in thee, the only All-Immaculate One.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The holy youths having shown themselves of old as for God in godliness, passed through the inconstant furnace flame as in a bridal chamber, singing with one accord the hymn: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

The Pre-Eternal One, keeping thy clean signs and whole through thine impassable gates, O Queen of all, [f. 4rA] preserved thee intact: pure even after childbirth. Therefore we cry out: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*

Cast into a furnace burning sevenfold with soul-destroying troubles, I have been scorched; but do thou thyself, O Sovereign Lady, Good One, rain down dew upon my horn by thy prayers, that I may cry out: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

I have grown old in passions, and worn away by unceasing assaults, and having reached the setting of my life, devoid of virtues, consumed by sloth, I cry unto thee, O Sovereign Lady: Comfort of mortals, have mercy on me!

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Serving the Trinity in unity in the Orthodox faith, and proclaiming thee in songs, O all-pure Mother, as having given birth to God in the flesh, we mortals divinely sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The furnace moist with dew showed of old the image of a wonder beyond nature, for the fire did not consume the young children, displaying the seedless, divine birth-giving of Christ from the Virgin; therefore, singing, let us sing: Let all creation bless the Lord, and exalt Him above all forever!

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

The co-essential Word prefigured thy childbirth to the Priest, O Virgin; for truly thou didst bear the Word of God, and the womb was not opened, O Virgin, through which passed [f. 4vA] God. Rejoicing in thee as our due, O Mother of God, with one accord we hymn and exalt thee, O Pure One, unto all ages.

*Troparion:*

Burn up, O All-Pure One, with divine fire the thorns that lie inert in my soul, and by thy prayers set me upon virtue, to bear fruit for Christ; for from thee the ever-living Flower has blossomed, adorning all creation. Therefore we honor thee, the pure Birthgiver of God, unto all ages.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Grant me speedily painless healing in cruel afflictions, O Birthgiver of God; for I have fallen into sorrows and troubles, and wretch that I am, I invoke thy swift protection with weeping. Therefore, O All-Pure One, hasten to rescue me, and save me from every torment, that I may sing of thy good childbirth.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Aaron's rod, which budded of old, prefigured thee, O Virgin; for thou alone didst bring forth an offshoot without man, having received the heavenly rain into thy womb. Therefore rejoicing in thee, O Theotokos, as our due, we all sing with one accord and exalt thee unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

The mystery of the Virgin is ineffable: for she was revealed as heaven, the Cherubic Throne, and the light-bearing bridal chamber of Christ God the Almighty; her do we all piously magnify as the Theotokos.

[f. 5rA] *Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Marvelous is the mystery of the Virgin: for He Whom the heavens above could not contain, she contained within her womb. Therefore, coming together, we bless her, and faithfully magnifying her, we magnify her.

*Troparion:*

Praising thee alone as higher than the heavens, the Divine Dawn, O Immaculate One, the Cherubic Throne and Bridal Chamber, and Holy Couch, we mortals magnify Christ our God, Whom thou didst bear from pure loins.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Many sorrows have defiled me, and evil temptations now assaulting me have painfully cast me into a pit of sins that have come upon me; therefore I pray thee in the bitterness of my soul, O Most Pure Theotokos, that I may find deliverance.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Reconcile the world, O Christ, through the prayers of the pure Maid of God, casting down the enemy's power beneath the feet of the King of the faithful; and establishing an ineffable peace for them, preserve it unto ages.

*Then: It is truly meet... Trisagion, and after Our Father... Hypakoe of the Tone. Lord, have mercy (40 times). Prayer: Thou Who at every time... Lord, have mercy (3 times). Glory... Both now... More honorable than the Cherubim... In the name of the Lord, Father, bless. Then the Prayer: O Spotless, undefiled... and the rest as usual, and the Dismissal.*

## ON SUNDAY AT SMALL COMPLINE (TONE 1)

[f. 5vA] *On Sunday at Small Compline,*[^10]

*As was indicated on Saturday at Small Compline.*

*Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*Ode 1, Tone 1. Heirmos:*

Thy victorious right hand has been divinely glorified in power; for it, O Immortal One, as all-powerful, shattered the adversaries, renewing for the Israelites a path through the deep.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Ineffably thou didst conceive God, and beyond nature didst bear Him, O All-Pure Sovereign Lady, Who delivered mortals from transgressions. Therefore I pray thee: deliver me from my many transgressions.

*Troparion:*

I am submerged in a gulf of sins and unnumbered troubles and sorrows; but by thy saving guidance and prayer, O All-Immaculate One, direct me unto the divine stillness of repentance.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Protectress of all my life, deliver me from many troubles, that I may sing unto thee a gracious voice, O pure Theotokos, and snatch me from future torment.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Still the assault of circumstances and the storm of temptations, O All-Immaculate Mother of the Deliverer, and [f. 6rA] Creator and our God, granting me the grace of salvation and purification.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou Who alone knowest the weakness of human nature, and didst mercifully clothe Thyself therein, gird me with power from on high to sing unto Thee, O Holy One: The animate Church of Thine ineffable glory, O Lover of mankind.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

The sight of thy form, O Pure One, is sweet unto angels, saving unto mortals, and terrible unto spirits of malice; which honoring with faith and kissing, O Theotokos, we enlighten our soul.

*Troparion:*

Not with thy virginal blood did the Creator and Lord clothe His flesh; pray Him, O All-Immaculate One, to have compassion upon me who am corrupted by boundless deeds, according to His ineffable mercy.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Having acquired thee as the Guardian of my life, an invincible rampart, a shelter of resting, an unconquered protectress, leading unto God, and bestowing eternal life: vouchsafe me to find this and glory, O All-Immaculate One.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

A seedless conception, O pure one, and an ineffable childbirth; for God was He Who was born, calling back the fall of human nature. Therefore we hymn thee as the true and real Theotokos.

[f. 6vA] *Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Habakkuk perceiving with foresighted eyes the mountain overshadowed by divine grace, proclaimed that the Holy One of Israel was to come forth from thee, for our salvation and renewal.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

An unrestrained impulse draws me unto evil by the working of the enemy and wicked habit; help me, O Sovereign Lady, lest the all-destroying one attack me utterly, preventing me by death from repentance.

*Troparion:*

I have spent all the time of my life in evil lusts and cruel troubles, and now the doers thereof lead my humble soul; but do thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, help me.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Hear my painful voice, O All-Immaculate One, which from the depth of my soul I bring forth with sighing, and grant me remission of the debts which I, wretch that I am, have committed in mind, being rightly devoid of understanding.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

O Sovereign Lady of the world, Theotokos, help me; for the all-wicked enemy desires vehemently to swallow me up by cruel memories and thoughts which separate me from God; therefore leave me not, neither despise me.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Thou Who didst enlighten the ends of the world with the radiance of Thy coming, O Christ, and didst make them resound by Thy Cross: enlighten with the light of Thy divine knowledge the hearts of those who hymn Thee in the Orthodox faith.

[f. 7rA] *Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Lo, I have acquired help ready unto salvation; therefore I fall down before thee and cry with tears: O Theotokos, deliver me from the snares of the alien, from the examination that is to come.

*Troparion:*

Thou art my strength, thou art my boast and rejoicing, and my guardian, protection and refuge, and invincible defender, O All-Pure Theotokos, Virgin; therefore save thy servant.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Cleanse my wretched soul defiled by passions with the sprinkling of thy divine mercy, O All-Pure Theotokos, granting it the radiant garment of thy salvation.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

In my life thou art my steadfast guidance, delivering me from the tempest of many circumstances; and at my departure, I pray thee, stand by me, saving me, O Mother of Christ God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

The lowest abyss has surrounded us, there is none to deliver, we are accounted as sheep for the slaughter; save Thy people, O our God, for Thou art the strength of the weak and their restoration.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

O all-honorable, all-pure Birthgiver of God, stretch forth thy hand unto me, and snatch me from the depth of evils which I, all-wretched, have brought upon my passionate soul by my folly.

[f. 7vA] Arise, O Pure One, unto the help of thy servant who has all his hope after God in thine unconquered protection, lest the enemy prevail, seize and destroy me.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Healer of the sick, resurrection of the fallen, cleansing of sinners, O Theotokos, I pray thee, falling down with tears, and cry: As thou art powerful, do thou thyself save me who am perishing.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Have mercy, O Pure One, upon my humble soul, anticipate and snatch it away, rescuing it from the wicked one; for he hastens to cast it into the abyss for the sake of the evils which my great sloth has wrought.

*Lord, have mercy (3 times). Glory... Both now... Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*

*Tone 1.*

*Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се):*

We all have acquired thee as a protectress before God, O Pure One, and we flee unto thy holy Church, asking help from thee continually, O Virgin; deliver therefore those who bless thee from the surrounding malice, and snatch them from the dreadful condemnation.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

We the faithful understand thee, O Theotokos, to be a spiritual furnace; for as the Most Exalted One saved the three youths, so has He Who is praised and supremely glorified, the God of our fathers, renewed the whole of me, man, in thy womb.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

[f. 8rA] *Troparion:*

Thou wast the divine Tabernacle, O Sovereign Lady: for having conceived in thy womb and borne Him in the flesh, thou only undefiled one, therefore deliver us from passions, diseases, sorrows, and debts; for thou hast great strength and power.

*Troparion:*

Still the unbearable sickness of my all-wretched soul, O Pure One, granting me true joy, O Sovereign Lady, and calming by thy prayers the wave of my many transgressions; for thou art my refuge and deliverance.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

I have placed all the hope of my salvation in thee, for I have acquired thee as help, an unconquerable tower, a foundation, and an assurance of steadfastness; and through thee, O Pure One, I hope to obtain mercy, O Sovereign Lady.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Thou wast the Orient of the Sun of glory, O Sovereign Lady, Pure One; for by His appearing through thee He delivered all from darkness, ignorance, and foul transgressions. Therefore I cry unto thee: Deliver me from the outer darkness.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

In the furnace the children of Israel, as in a crucible, shone with the beauty of godliness purer than gold, saying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, sing and exalt Him above all forever.

*(Manuscript inscription at foot: "Belongs to the library of the Trinity-St. Sergius Lavra. In the year 1859.")*[^11]

[f. 8vA] *Troparion:*

The turmoils of passions cruelly afflict my soul; but having borne the Cause and Giver of peace and stillness, O Pure Theotokos, keeping me in a peaceful disposition, fill me with serene joy and gladness.

*Troparion:*

Thou hast been shown unto us as the Mediatress of salvation, O Theotokos, who didst bear the Savior and the Good of all things; therefore I pray thee, vouchsafe salvation unto my humble soul, that I may faithfully hymn thee unto all ages.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Having ineffably conceived the Creator of all and God: save me from every temptation, O Maiden, and grant me to cry continually: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, sing and exalt Him above all forever.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst bear the Creator and God of all, O Virgin Mother; pray Him to grant purification of transgressions, deliverance from sorrows and temptations, and freedom from eternal fire unto those who unceasingly glorify thy glory.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

The burning bush showed the image of thy pure childbirth unburnt; and now we pray thee to quench the furnace of temptations raging against us, that we may unceasingly magnify thee, O Theotokos.

[f. 9rA] *Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

O all-immaculate Mother of God, open unto me thy merciful womb, and deliver me from the jaws of the intelligible wolf who seeks to destroy and corrupt me; have mercy, I pray thee: turn not away thy servant put to shame.

*Troparion:*

Be thou the Protectress and firm defense of all my life; and deliver me from many temptations, sorrows, malicious tongues of enemies, and eternal fire, O All-Immaculate One.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Thou alone art the visitation of the sick, thou alone art the restoration of the fallen, O Sovereign Lady, thou alone art the departure leading unto God, thou alone art the Mediatress of eternal blessings: have mercy upon me who alone have sinned more than all.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Receive my tearful prayer now, and grant me remission of transgressions and of many evils which I have done, O All-Hymned Lady Theotokos, that I may not perish utterly from boundless sin.

*Then: It is truly meet... and the rest as usual, and the Dismissal.*

## ON MONDAY AT SMALL COMPLINE (TONE 1)

[f. 9vA] *On Monday at Small Compline,*[^12]

*Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*Ode 1, Tone 1. Heirmos:*

Let us all sing a song of victory unto God Who wrought marvelous wonders with a high arm, and saved Israel, for He has been glorified.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

All-pure cleansing, rejoice, O Virgin, adornment of angels and receptacle of the Spirit, immaculate Bride of the unoriginate Father, and Mother of the Word of God!

*Troparion:*

Thou art noble as the King's daughter, having blossomed forth, O Lady Theotokos, and having borne the King and God in the flesh, and reigning with Him.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

David the singer hymns thy childbirth, and Isaiah supremely praises thy most pure womb, O all-immaculate Mary; and the Christian people glorify thy childbirth.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

The Church proclaims thy childbirth, O Virgin Bride of God, with Orthodox teachings and divine deeds, for it venerates the icon of the incarnation of thy Son.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

The stone which the builders rejected has become the head of the corner; this is the stone whereon Christ has established His Church, which He redeemed from the nations.

[f. 10rA] *Troparion:*

Having the glory of virginity, thou hast been clothed with the grace of the Holy Spirit, O All-Pure Mary, having appeared unto God as a new and hidden mystery: for how dost thou remain a virgin having borne God in the flesh?

*Troparion:*

The deed which Thou hast wrought, O Lord, who shall declare? For Thou Who didst condemn woman to bear children in sorrows hast appeared as her Son; she rejoices, woman's fallen nature, in Thee, O God, because of the Theotokos.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

The storm of transgressions is loosed for the world through thee, O All-Pure Mary, united unto God alone; therefore having thee as a harbor of salvation, we the faithful praise thee, singing thy greatness.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Thy virginity was hymned by prophets of old, that thou shouldest give birth in time unto the Timeless One, the Word beyond word, One of the Trinity, Christ our God, Whose icons the Church honors, O pure Mother.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Foreseeing in the Spirit the incarnation of the Word, the Prophet Habakkuk proclaimed, crying out: When the years draw near, Thou shalt be known; when the time comes, Thou shalt appear; glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*

The seven-branched mirror of the divine prophets hymns thy prefigurations in many ways; and we, having received the reality of the sign of thine exodus, seeing the thing itself, truly believe in thee as the Mother of God.

[f. 10vA] *Troparion:*

Worthily honoring thee in truth as the Mother of God, O pure Virgin, we glorify thee, bringing unto thee our own voice, unto her who was proclaimed in the Law and the Prophets: Rejoice, thou who art full of grace, the Lord is with thee!

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

O divine Mountain, which Habakkuk saw, clearly overshadowing with virtues, O Theotokos, budding forth in the world: through which, having departed of old through transgression, we have again drawn near unto God.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Blessed is the tongue, O all-immaculate and pure one, which is vouchsafed to honor thy Childbirth faithfully with Orthodox teachings and deeds, manifesting the truth, all the prefigurations of the incarnation of thy Son.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Rising early from the night, we praise Thee, O Christ God, Who becamest poor for our sake, and suffered crucifixion and death in the flesh.

*Troparion:*

Hiddenly and mystically the unburnt Bush revealed thee unto Moses, O Sovereign Lady Theotokos; for the womb of material fire did not burn, containing the Godhead.

*Troparion:*

Hymning thee as the Mother of God the Word, O pure Theotokos, we offer thee a rational, blossoming gift, wherein the faithful rejoice.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Turn not away praises from defiled lips, O all-immaculate Theotokos; for there is none upon earth who [f. 11rA] dares to hymn thee worthily.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

He Who was born of the Virgin is One in two natures, as invisible God, and as man, having voluntarily become like unto men for our sake.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Thou didst save the Prophet from the whale, O Lover of mankind; and bring me up from the depth of transgressions, I pray Thee.

*Troparion:*

The Prophet sees thee, O Virgin, as an intelligible seven-flamed lampstand, clearly showing the workings of the Spirit gleaming within thee.

*Troparion:*

Thou didst appear, O Virgin, truly as a life-bearing tree, putting to death the deceitful serpent by thy fruit, as having given birth to Life, Christ God.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

A praise without sorrow is offered unto thee from defiled lips, O pure Mother of our God; but behold the longing of my soul, and accept this.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

The lawless are shaken in the turmoil of bitter unbelief, whensoever they behold the assemblies of the Orthodox kissing thine icon, O Virgin.

*Lord, have mercy (3 times). Glory... Both now... Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*

*Tone 1.*

*Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се):*

Pilot my wretched soul, O Pure One, and have compassion upon it, O All-Immaculate One, which has slipped into many [f. 11vA] transgressions into the abyss of destruction; and at the dreadful hour of death, do thou snatch me away from the slandering demons and from every torment.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

When the fire in the furnace preserved Thy children untouched, O Savior, then the three, as with one mouth, sang and blessed, saying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*

Thou didst appear adorned with virtues, O Bride of God, with the glory and honor of virginity; most comely indeed is thy beauty in magnificence; truly the Lord, the Lover, took thee unto Himself as a divine Mother.

*Troparion:*

Thou wast clearly the rod of uprightness, O Pure One, the most honorable scepter of the Kingdom. Therefore thou didst bud forth the Fruit, God the Word, which wonder Aaron's rod clearly prefigured from afar.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

With many bodily passions I have defiled both body and soul, wretched as I am; how shall I hymn the beauty of thy virtues, O Pure One? I am at a loss and terrified; but do thou thyself be help unto me by thy prayers.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

I honor the union of Thy Incarnation, O Christ, and I bow down before the icon thereof in a manner befitting God, for [f. 12rA] being God pre-eternal, from the Virgin Thou becamest man without change, having Thyself united both.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Him at Whom angels and all hosts tremble as Creator and Lord, sing, ye priests; glorify, ye children; bless, ye peoples; and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*

Let the divine mystery be hymned in a manner befitting God, whereby the world was delivered from corruption. Arise, all ye who of old had fallen: for Mary gave birth to Christ, salvation unto all!

*Troparion:*

We hymn thee, O all-immaculate Mother of God; and who does not call thee blessed who art truly good, being the hope of our souls? Therefore, O All-Pure One, accept my supplication.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Let the faithful be clothed today in songs; glorify, ye angels and poor together: for the devil falls wounded, for Mary gives birth unto the hope of salvation.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Circumscribe not the Godhead, lie not, ye blind ones; for simply it is invisible and unseeable; but depicting the form of the Flesh, I bow down, and with faith I glorify the Virgin who gave birth to the Lord.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

The light-bearing cloud, wherein the Master of all descended from heaven like dew upon the fleece, and became incarnate for our sake, He Who is without beginning having become man: all of us magnify thee as the pure Mother of our God.

[f. 12vA] *Troparion:*

Rejoice, O Mary, wonder of creation in magnificence! Rejoice, daughter of David and Mother of the Lord! Rejoice, song of Gabriel! Rejoice, refuge and confirmation, and help unto all sinners upon earth, O All-Immaculate One!

*Troparion:*

Marvelous Joachim and divine Anna sing by the Divine Spirit and chant a song of thanksgiving, beholding upon earth the Mother of the Creator, daughter Mary, whom they bore; through her prayers, have compassion upon us, as God!

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

O all-immaculate Virgin, Mother of God the Most High, by thy childbirth salvation appeared upon earth unto sinners and the humble; for believing in thee we were saved; but do thou save me also now, and unto all ages of my life.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

I know the Word to be incarnate, yet unchangeable; therefore I faithfully hymn Him as having two natures, yet one Hypostasis, Which I clearly saw as man; and preaching this one Hypostasis, I finish my song.

*Then: It is truly meet... and the rest as usual, and the Dismissal.*

## ON TUESDAY AT SMALL COMPLINE (TONE 1)

[f. 13rA] *On Tuesday at Small Compline,*[^13]

*Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*Ode 1, Tone 1. Heirmos:*

Thy victorious right hand has been divinely glorified in power; for it, O Immortal One, as all-powerful, shattered the adversaries, renewing for the Israelites a path through the deep.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Truly the Incomprehensible One of heaven, O all-immaculate Maiden, took flesh from thy flesh, and renewed that which had decayed by the first transgression.

*Troparion:*

Let us the faithful bless in songs the Mother of God the Word beyond word; for this all-immaculate one appeared as the adornment of mortals and became the Mediatress of all who have sinned.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Thou didst appear more spacious than the heavens, having in every way contained the Creator, O Mother and Ever-Virgin. Therefore I cry unto thee: Deliver me from every death-bearing narrowness, O thou who art full of divine joy!

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Grant us help, O Pure One, who are tempest-tossed by the tumult of sorrows; cast down our enemies' [f. 13vA] wicked exaltations, and grant salvation unto all who piously glorify thee.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou Who alone knowest the weakness of human nature, and didst mercifully clothe Thyself therein: gird me with power from on high to sing unto Thee, O Holy One: The animate Church of Thine ineffable glory, O Lover of mankind.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

He Who is full empties Himself for our sake, and He Who is without beginning receives a beginning from thee, O all-immaculate Virgin; and He Who is invisible is seen, and He Who nourishes all things is nourished with milk, providing to renew mankind.

*Troparion:*

Heal our contrition, O Virgin unwedded, having given birth to the Healer and Lord, Who showed us an entrance unto life by grace; pray Him unceasingly to have compassion and save thy servants.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Thou alone didst deify human nature beyond nature, O All-Immaculate One, by Him Who was born of thee; pray Him to grant forgiveness of our transgressions and eternal rejoicing, O pure, all-hymned Virgin.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Living daily in sloth, lo, I fall down before thee, O All-Immaculate One: by thy continual prayers unto God, raise up my mind, and enlighten it, blackened by sloth, with the radiance of repentance, that with faith and love I may glorify thee.

[f. 14rA] *Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Habakkuk perceiving with foresighted eyes the mountain overshadowed by divine grace, proclaimed that the Holy One of Israel was to come forth from thee, for our salvation and renewal.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

We know thee as the divine Palace of the Word, wherein He dwelt in the flesh, and renewed us who had decayed through passions. Therefore, O Pure One, we honor thee as the Mother of God, and we glorify Him Who was born of thee.

*Troparion:*

Appearing in thy womb, O Virgin, the Word, having become incarnate and dwelt therein, drove out the curse that was previously in human nature, and divinely renewed His own by divine union; for Whose sake we hymn thee.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Trouble seized Adam, O All-Immaculate One, when he tasted of the hidden fruit; but now by thy Childbirth he has revived and is established in the dwelling of paradise. For this cause we worthily hymn thee.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst stop the flow of death, having given birth to the Fount of immortality, Christ God, O Pure One; pray Him therefore, O thou who art full of divine grace, to heal the death-bearing passions of my soul, and to save me.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Thou Who didst enlighten the ends of the world with the radiance of Thy coming, O Christ, and didst make them resound by Thy Cross: enlighten with the light of Thy divine knowledge the hearts of those who hymn Thee in the Orthodox faith.

[f. 14vA] *Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

From thy womb the unsetting Sun shone forth, O All-Immaculate One, and destroyed the darkness utterly, and enlightened the earth with divine understanding; therefore we faithfully hymn thee.

*Troparion:*

Rejoice! we cry unto thee with the angelic voice, O Pure One; for thou didst bear the Angel of Great Counsel, co-unoriginate with the Father, Who became flesh, O Virgin, in order to save mankind.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

The Lord dwelt in thy womb, O Pure One, and has delivered from the dominion of the enemy all those who piously take strength in Him; therefore with a loud voice we all hymn thee, O all-immaculate Lady.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

The Master clothed Himself in me, Who came forth from thee, O All-Immaculate One, and deified corrupted nature; pray Him, O Mother of God, to strip me bare of every sin.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

The lowest abyss has surrounded us, and there is none to deliver; we are accounted as sheep for the slaughter; save Thy people, O our God, for Thou art the strength of the weak and their restoration.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Without seed thou didst conceive God in thy womb, and after thy marvelous Childbirth thou didst remain a Virgin, O All-Immaculate One; therefore with praises we glorify thee, who through thee, O All-Pure One, have been delivered from the curse.

[f. 15rA] *Troparion:*

The Ladder which Jacob saw, the Overshadowed Mountain, the Radiant Cloud of Divine Light, the Door through which God alone passed, blessed among women: thou art she, O All-Immaculate One!

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Apart from natural laws, O Virgin unwedded, thou didst give birth to the hypostatic Word of God Who heals our contrition; pray Him diligently to save all of us.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Bringing my soul wounded by transgressions, I cry unto thee, O All-Hymned, Most Glorious and Pure One: heal and save me by thy mediation, that I may hymn and magnify thy protection.

*Lord, have mercy (3 times). Glory... Both now... Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*

Having acquired thy protection, O All-Pure One, and being delivered from perils by thy prayers, preserved at all times by the Cross of thy Son, we all worthily and piously magnify thee.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

We the faithful understand thee, O Theotokos, to be a spiritual furnace; for as the Most Exalted One saved the three youths, so has He Who is praised and supremely glorified, the God of our fathers, renewed the whole of me, man, in thy womb.

[f. 15vA] *Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

The shadow of the Law and the ancient enigmas of the God-proclaiming Prophets clearly proclaimed beforehand thy seedless Childbirth, O all-immaculate and pure Lady; which hymning, we praise the supremely hymned and glorified God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*

Thou didst give birth to the Orient of the Sun, O Pure One full of divine grace, Who enlightened the whole fullness of the faithful, and put an end to the night of impiety; therefore we honor the supremely hymned and glorified God of our fathers.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

The unburnt Bush of old prefigured thy womb, O All-Immaculate One: the fire of the Godhead did not burn thee at all; therefore I pray thee: deliver me from the unquenchable fire, that unceasingly, O Pure One, I may hymn thy greatness.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

O Mother of God, help of the faithful, cleansing of sinners, Bestower of all good things: accept me who fall down before thy compassionate mercy, coming unto thee with weeping, and asking remission of my sins.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

In the furnace the children of Israel, as in a crucible, shone with the beauty of godliness purer than gold, saying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, sing and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

[f. 16rA] *Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

The legal prefigurations and the ancient enigmas, O Virgin, clearly foretold thee as the Pure Mother; and we, now beholding the reality of things, hymn thee with one accord and glorify thee unto all ages.

*Troparion:*

Adorned with divine beauties, having given birth in beauty without passion to God the Word, O All-Immaculate One, enlightening the hearts of all with divine splendors, of those who praise Him in faith unto all ages.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

With sacred voices the Prophets told from afar that thou shouldest be the Mother of God the Most High, O All-Immaculate One, unto Whom we all cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, sing and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

How beautiful, how all-comely art thou, O Maiden, who without blame didst bear Christ, the Most Comely in beauties, unto Whom we all cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, sing and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

The burning bush showed the image of thy pure childbirth unburnt; and now we pray thee to quench the furnace of temptations raging against us, that we may unceasingly magnify thee, O Theotokos.

[f. 16vA] *Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

The nature of mortals, which was previously condemned, O Virgin, by thy supernatural divine Childbirth has obtained immortality and received its former beauty, hymning thee with one accord in joyous songs.

*Troparion:*

Thou bearest Him Who bears all things, and thou feedest with milk Him Who gives food unto all. Great and awesome is thy mystery beyond understanding, O Ark of the venerable priesthood, Virgin Theotokos; therefore we all bless thee.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Shine forth light unto me who lie in the darkness of sloth, O Pure One, dispelling the blinding thoughts of passions, and continually working deep peace in my soul, O All-Immaculate One, that I may glorify thee.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

I tremble at the unbribable judgment-seat and the unforgetting Judge, O All-Immaculate One; for I possess an unnumbered multitude of sins, living carelessly in sloth; therefore, O Theotokos, have compassion upon me and have mercy on me.

*Then: It is truly meet... Trisagion, and after Our Father... and the rest as usual, and the Dismissal.*

## ON WEDNESDAY AT SMALL COMPLINE (TONE 1)

[f. 17rA] *On Wednesday at Small Compline,*[^14]

*Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*Ode 1, Tone 1. Heirmos:*

Let us all sing a song of victory unto God Who wrought marvelous wonders with a high arm, and saved Israel, for He has been glorified.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

The Bush prefigured thee, O Birthgiver of God; for thou didst truly bear the restless fire, remaining unburnt; therefore with faithful voices we ever praise thee in song.

*Troparion:*

God the Word clothed Himself in man and was incarnate from thee beyond comprehension, O All-Pure One; therefore every breath glorifies thee, and bows down before thee, and worthily honors thee.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Thou didst conceive the ineffable Word, O Pure One, Who holds all the ends of the earth, and Him didst thou bear; pray Him diligently to have mercy on us.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

O thou who alone didst bear the timeless God incarnate in time, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady: heal the timeless passions of my passionate soul.

[f. 17vA] *Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Let my heart be established in Thy will, O Christ God, Who didst establish the second heaven above the waters, and didst found the earth upon the waters, O All-Powerful One.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

That He might deify humanity, God became man from thee, O pure Virgin, beyond word and understanding; for this cause we the faithful bless thee with one accord.

*Troparion:*

He Who is uncircumscribed by nature, having become incarnate from thee was circumscribed, O Pure One full of divine grace; pray Him unceasingly to have compassion and enlighten the souls of those who call thee blessed.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

From my mind which has been barren, drive away all unfruitfulness, and show my soul to be fruitful in virtues, O pure Theotokos, helper of the faithful.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Deliver me, O All-Immaculate One, from every circumstance, and from the many serpent's snares, from eternal fire and darkness, thou who didst bear the Unsetting Light.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Foreseeing in the Spirit the incarnation of the Word, the Prophet Habakkuk proclaimed, saying: When the years draw near, Thou shalt be known; when the time comes, Thou shalt appear; glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Christ dwelt in thy pure womb, O all-holy Sovereign Lady, and deified us, having taken ensouled flesh.

[f. 18rA] Therefore we piously hymn thee as the Pure Mother, O Sovereign Lady, helper of the world.

*Troparion:*

Holy Theotokos, sanctify us, who didst bear in the flesh the All-Pure One, Who willed to become like unto men; and by thy prayers, O All-Immaculate One, show all of us to be partakers of the Heavenly Kingdom.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

O Virgin Theotokos, undefiled Tabernacle, cleanse me who am defiled with transgressions by the pure rays of all thy compassions, and give me a hand of help, that I may cry: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst appear as a consecrated church unto God Who dwelt in thee beyond understanding, O Virgin; pray Him to cleanse the defilement of our sins, that we may be shown as temples and habitations of the Spirit.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Grant us Thy peace, O Son of God; for other than Thee we know no god, we name Thy Name, for Thou art the God of the living and of the dead.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Of old in Eden the wicked tasting wrought my death; but having given birth to Life, O Pure One, quicken me who was dead of old by the tree, that glorifying thee I may hymn thee.

[f. 18vA] *Troparion:*

Save me, O All-Immaculate One, from cruel perils, raise me up from festering passions, and deliver me from the captivity and malice of wicked demons, who honor thee with love.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

We know thee as Cloud, and Paradise, and Door of Light, Table, and Fleece, and Jar containing the Manna, the sweetness of the world, O pure Virgin Mother.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Having given birth, O All-Immaculate One, to God Emmanuel, Who visibly became man out of tender mercy: pray Him as the Lover of mankind, O Pure One, to have compassion upon the people who have sinned.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Like the Prophet Jonah, bring up my life from corruption, O Christ God, I cry unto Thee, O Merciful One; for life is with Thee, salvation and power.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

I pray thee, who art the good and undefiled Tabernacle, cleanse me who am defiled with many sins from every stain by thy mediation.

*Troparion:*

Be a pilot unto me, O Pure One, who am tossed by the tempest in the cruel deep of the assaults of this life, and guide me unto the harbor of salvation, and save me.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

The surge of thoughts, the assaults of passions, and the depth of sins weigh down my wretched soul; help me, O holy Sovereign Lady!

*Both now...*

[f. 19rA] *Theotokion:*

O Mary, prefigured tabernacle of sanctification: sanctify my wretched soul defiled by passions, and make me a partaker of divine glory.

*Lord, have mercy (3 times). Glory... Both now... Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*

*Tone 1.*

*Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се):*

O Virgin and Nourisher of One of the Trinity, adorned Paradise, salvation of mortals: save by thy protection those who piously hymn thee; for thou didst bear Him Who spoke through the Prophets. Thou didst carry Him Who upholds all things, as Mother of Christ God.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

When the fire in the furnace preserved Thy children untouched, O Savior, then the three, as with one mouth, sang and blessed, saying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

The Son of the unoriginate Father, dwelling in thy womb, received a beginning, that as God He might deliver us from the wicked rulers of darkness, O pure Birthgiver of God, who bow down before Him.

*Troparion:*

Adorned with divine virtues, O pure Virgin, thou didst bear the Word, co-unoriginate with the Father, Who truly covered the heavens with virtues; pray Him ever to have compassion upon us.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Sanctify our thoughts, strengthen the souls of all, O Mother of God, rightly to do righteousness: through tender mercy [f. 19vA] of the Word of the unoriginate Father, Who was incarnate from thee, O Virgin, out of ineffable compassion.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Weighed down by many passions, O All-Immaculate One, quicken my mind and strengthen me to do things pleasing unto God, that I may magnify thee ever as the Protectress of Christians and their hope.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Him at Whom angels and all hosts tremble as Creator and Lord, sing, ye priests; glorify, ye children; bless, ye peoples; and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

We are delivered, O All-Immaculate One, by thy vigilant prayers unto God, unharmed in every way, knowing thee as the Theotokos, Blessed and Full of Divine Joy!

*Troparion:*

He Who is bodiless becomes incarnate from thee in a manner befitting God; pray Him, O All-Pure One, to put to death the passions of my flesh, and to quicken my soul which is being slain by sins.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Thou didst bear the Savior and God Who healed the contrition of earthy Adam, O All-Pure One; pray Him to heal the wounds of my soul which are incurably sick.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Raise up me who lie in the depth of evils, and conquer the enemies who now war against me, who have consumed my soul with improper pleasures; despise me not, O Pure One, but have compassion and save me.

[f. 20rA] *Ode 9. Heirmos:*

The light-bearing cloud, wherein the Master of all descended from heaven like dew upon the fleece, and became incarnate for our sake, He Who is without beginning having become man: all of us magnify thee as the pure Mother of our God.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Having given birth to the Divine Light Which shone forth from the Father, O All-Immaculate One, have compassion upon my soul which is darkened by the pleasures of life and has become a laughingstock unto enemies; and vouchsafe it the light of saving repentance, O Pure One.

*Troparion:*

Isaiah beheld thee as a light-resembling cloud, O All-Immaculate One, from which the Sun of righteousness, having shone forth upon us, mystically enlightened creation; therefore in faith we hymn thee, good among women.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Being a lover of sin and living in sloth, I tremble at the unbribable judgment-seat, O Pure One; whereat preserve me uncondemned by thy holy prayers, O Virgin Bride of God, that I may ever bless thee as my Protectress.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

I tremble at the judgment-seat, O Virgin, and at the unforgetting Eye of thy Son, having committed many sins upon earth; therefore I cry unto thee: O all-merciful Sovereign Lady, help me, and rescue me uncondemned from that future distress, O Pure One.

*Then: It is truly meet... Trisagion, and after Our Father... and the rest as usual, and the Dismissal.*

## ON THURSDAY AT SMALL COMPLINE (TONE 1)

[f. 20vA] *On Thursday at Small Compline,*[^15]

*Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*Ode 1, Tone 1. Heirmos:*

Having delivered Israel from cruel bondage, and led them through the impassable deep as on dry land, seeing the enemy drowned, they sing a song unto God in rejoicing, Who works wonders with a high arm: for He has been glorified.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Held fast by despair, contemplating the multitude of my transgressions and the answer to be given unto the Judge, O Sovereign Lady Theotokos: do thou become my divine Mediatress, altering His wrath, regarding me with mercy.

*Troparion:*

Christian Refuge, Restoration of those who fall, O All-Pure One, Cleansing of those who sin: deliver me from the dread judgment and the unquenchable fire, granting me eternal life.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Thee alone all we the faithful have acquired as a steadfast Protectress; for thou didst bear God; therefore with praises, O Pure One, all earthly generations bless thee according to thy word.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Beholding the Creator and thy Son upon the Cross, O All-Immaculate One, filled with awe thou didst say: What is this, O Child, that the wicked have rendered Thee these things for the good things which Thou didst manifest unto them?

[f. 21rA] *Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Let not the mortal boast in his own wisdom nor in riches, but in the faith of the Lord, crying in Orthodox manner unto Christ God, and singing ever: Establish me upon the rock of Thy commandments, O Master.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

All the Prophets proclaimed thee as the Mother of God by marvelous types; seeing the fulfillment whereof clearly, we together with thee ask to obtain divine stillness through thee.

*Troparion:*

Sovereign Lady of the world, salvation and protection of the faithful: despise not the unceasing sighs of my heart from the depth, and having received streams of tears, loose and save me who am bound by many transgressions.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Having boldness unto Christ God as a Mother, O Pure One, pray ever that we may be delivered from the Hagarene children and from every harm, and strengthen us, O All-Immaculate One, thankfully to glorify Him.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Beholding her own Son lifted up upon the Cross, the All-Pure One cried aloud, and in heartache exclaimed, crying: O Son, what have the wicked and lawless done unto Thee, who were filled with Thy gifts!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Of old Habakkuk heard Thy marvelous report, O Christ, and with fear cried aloud: God shall come from Theman, and the Holy One from the mountain overshadowed by the dense forest, to save His anointed ones; glory to Thy power, O Lord!

[f. 21vA] *Troparion:*

Cast down the pride of the subtle enemy and the wiles brought against me, and clothe me with thy strength, O All-Immaculate One, preserving me ever unharmed, pure, and unshaken, who radiantly hymn thee.

*Troparion:*

Vanquished by the law of the flesh, all-passionate I commit improper iniquities, and in no way dare to raise mine eye unto thee, O Pure Sovereign Lady; but by the law of thy tender mercy, save me, the wretched one, save me!

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Thee alone after God do we have as an end, a hope unashamed, and a protection, O Sovereign Lady; by thy prayers deliver us from all enemies, visible and invisible, from harm and temptations, that we may unceasingly glorify thee.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Seeing the Son Whom thou didst bear without seed nailed to the Cross, the All-Immaculate One cried aloud as a Mother, and weeping said: What new and marvelous and incomprehensible wonder do I see happening unto Thee, O my Son!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Rising early at night, we praise Thee, O Christ God, Who becamest poor for our sake, and didst suffer Crucifixion and death in the flesh.

[f. 22rA] *Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Having a sleepless prayer and fervent protection for thy servants, anticipating them in all the perils of life that fall upon them, O Virgin, snatch us away and save us, that we may not be swallowed up by sorrow and waste away.

*Troparion:*

O Mary, Dwelling of God: by repentance show me to be a dwelling of God, who through foul deeds have become a dwelling of wicked spirits, foolishly fulfilling their will.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Unto us who are grievously sick together in soul and body with sinful passions, grant healing as thou art merciful, O Mother of God; for thou didst truly give birth to Christ, the great Physician of souls and bodies, the inexhaustible Fount of life.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Seeing her Son upon the Wood of the Cross, the All-Immaculate One was torn in her inward parts, and with tears cried aloud: I am filled with awe at Thy long-suffering, O my Son, beholding a new wonder: how Thou, being sinless, dost endure an unjust death!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Cry with salvation from the sea monster, O Lover of mankind, and bring me up from the depth of sins, I pray Thee.

*Troparion:*

Thou didst become the receptacle of the spiritual myrrh which filled the whole earth with divine fragrance, [f. 22vA] *Troparion (continued):*

O All-Pure Bride of God; therefore by the sweet fragrance of thy prayer, destroy all the foul stench from the transgressions of my soul.

*Troparion:*

The fire of passions burns me greatly and afflicts my humble heart, and unlawfully joins me unto improper deeds; make haste to quench this, O thou who didst give birth to the Divine Fire, my Salvation, Bride of God!

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Deliver us, O All-Hymned Virgin, from the assault of enemies visible and invisible, and preserve us, O Mother of God, who truly confess thee as the Theotokos in the Orthodox faith; for thou ever hast the power, as having given birth to the Creator of all.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Standing by the Cross and seeing her Son hanging in the flesh, the All-Immaculate One was consumed with sorrow in her inward parts, and overflowing with tears she cried aloud: O Child, truly ineffable is Thy tender mercy toward all men!

*Lord, have mercy (3 times). Glory... Both now... Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*

*Tone 1.*

*Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*

Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd hanging dead upon the Tree, the Undefiled Ewe cried aloud weeping, exclaiming as a Mother: How shall I endure Thy condescension which is beyond word, O my Son, and Thy voluntary sufferings, O Most Good God?

[f. 23rA] *Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The youths nurtured in the faith of godliness took no heed of the ungodly command, and feared not the threat of fire, but standing in the midst of the flame they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

I cannot endure the assault and tumult of the demons, for the flame of carnal pleasure darkens my mind. But despise me not, O Most Holy Theotokos, who have placed my hope upon thee.

*Troparion:*

O Unwedded Virgin, All-Holy Sovereign Lady and Bride of God, having loosed the bonds of my transgressions by thy prayers, unite me to the yoke of Christ's love, that I may bring forth virtuous fruits.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Thou art the Protectress of all who are in the world, the Rampart of Christians, and a Refuge unashamed, O Pure Mother; therefore honoring thee in faith, we cry unto Christ: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Seeing her Son hanging upon the Cross, the All-Pure Virgin was filled with awe and wonder, and said: How shall I endure seeing Thee slain, the Prince of life and Giver of life!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

A great wonder was prefigured by the dew-dropping furnace in an image: as it received them, it did not burn the youths, even so neither did the Divine Fire burn the Virgin into whom He entered pure; therefore singing we cry aloud: Let all creation bless the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

[f. 23vA] *Troparion:*

Sovereign Lady of the world, Theotokos: bring up me who am utterly perished and sunken in the depth of evil mind, through carnal pleasures and attachment to worldly things, by thy tender mercy alone; for I have no hope of salvation whatsoever, being completely cast into despair, O Pure One.

*Troparion:*

Freely art thou the Savior of men, who didst bear God, O Ineffable Image! Thou art the Savior of the faithful, O Theotokos, and the Guide of the blind, and the Restoration of those who fall. Therefore praising thee, we cry unto Christ: Bless the Lord, all ye works, sing and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Having acquired thee as a most firm rampart, we have placed the hope of our salvation upon thee, O Mother of God; but be thou a haven unto thy servants, and an insurmountable wall, and direct us unceasingly to sing: Let all creation bless the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Torn in maternal inward parts and filled with many tears, she who purely bore Thee, seeing Thee upon the Cross, cried aloud with inconsolable sighs: Having escaped travails at Thy Nativity, O my Son, now am I held fast by anguish, seeing Thine appearance dishonored!

[f. 24rA] *Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Ineffable is the Mystery of the Virgin: for she was shown to be the Heaven, the Cherubic Throne, and the Light-Bearing Bridal Chamber of Christ God the Almighty; whom in godliness as the Theotokos we all magnify.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Wretched as I am, I have defiled my soul with the passions of dishonor, and defiled my flesh with soul-destroying pleasures; but as thou art Pure and Undefiled, cleanse me by the multitude of thy mercies.

*Troparion:*

I have acquired no refuge besides thee, O Pure One; I know no other firm protection and shelter upon earth, O Sovereign Lady. Therefore I have fervently fled unto thee, asking through thee to receive deliverance from transgressions.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Look down, O Pure One, now mercifully regarding thy servants from on high, preserving them in pious faith, and by thy prayers delivering us from all tribulations, who honor thee as the true and Pure Theotokos.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Knowing the seedless Birthgiving, when she beheld Thee, O Lover of mankind, hanging upon the Tree of the Cross, she cried aloud: O my Son and my All-Powerful God, desiring to save mankind, Thou hast now accepted the Crucifixion!

*Then: It is truly meet... Trisagion, and after Our Father... and the Dismissal.*

## ON FRIDAY AT SMALL COMPLINE (TONE 1)

[f. 24vA] *On Friday at Small Compline,*[^16]

*Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*Ode 1, Tone 1. Heirmos:*

Let us all sing a song of victory unto God Who wrought marvelous wonders with a high arm, and saved Israel: for He has been glorified.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Thou art a saving haven and protection unto those who call upon thee, O All-Pure Theotokos. Therefore I also call upon thee fervently from my soul: O Sovereign Lady, save me!

*Troparion:*

O Mary All-Pure, Mother of the Creator: heal my humble soul which has wasted away with passions and transgressions, as thou art acceptable unto God.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

O Unwedded Sovereign Lady, depart not, praying ever unto my Creator and God on behalf of him who ever flees unto thy protection, that he may obtain mercy.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Animate Palace of the Womb, and Fire-Resembling Throne: together with the Holy Martyrs and the Apostles, pray Christ unceasingly to deliver us from tribulations.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
