*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

A new and divinely-befitting wonder: for the closed virginal portal the Lord manifestly passes through, naked at His entry, and bearing flesh [f. 58vA] *Heirmos (continued):*

God appeared at His going forth, and the portal remains closed: her unceasingly, as the Mother of God, do we magnify!

*Troparion:*

From a holy root, a Holy Fruit: for unto barren parents cleansed by the Law of God, the Maiden budded forth, bringing forth the undefiled Life, the Theotokos; and Anna rejoices, having received in old age the Child, the Mother of God, whom we glorify.

*Troparion:*

New and God-revealing is the childbearing of thy holy and God-bearing womb, O Pure One: for therein by the Father's finger the Holy Son was inscribed as an image in man, incarnate by the Holy Spirit: Whom indivisibly as God and man we magnify.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Every judgment cries out against me: for the deeds of sinful actions raise a menacing cry against me, and all my soul cries out, convicted by them, and trembles before the flame of Gehenna: from which before the end, deliver me by thy prayers, O Sovereign Lady.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

After childbirth thou wast revealed incorrupt, O Pure One: for unchangeably didst thou, O Theotokos, bear in the flesh the Creator of all, man beyond nature, without departing from the essence of the Father; and again thou didst remain a Virgin by the Divine Spirit: therefore glorifying thee, we offer this song.

*Then, It is truly meet: Trisagion: and the rest as usual, and the Dismissal.*

## ON SUNDAY AT SMALL COMPLINE (TONE 3)

[f. 59rA] *On Sunday at Small Compline,*[^35]

*Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*Ode 1, Tone 3. Heirmos:*

He Who of old by divine nod gathered the waters into one gathering and divided the sea for the people of Israel, this is our God, exceedingly glorified: unto Him alone let us sing, for He has been glorified.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

O only One of great renown, Most Holy Virgin Mary, more glorious than the Cherubim, Daughter of David and Beauty of Jacob, Mother of our God: exceedingly magnified is thy glory, and all we praise thee with faith.

*Troparion:*

Him Who by His word stretched out the heavens like a curtain, thou didst carry in thine arms as a man: implore Him unchangeably, O Pure One, to deliver my soul from irrational passions, and from every affliction.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Foreordained before all ages, O Virgin, Most Pure Purple of the King of Glory, in which He clothed Himself in the last times: cover the coats of skins of our forefather Adam, and cover me now.

*Both now...*

[f. 59vA] *Theotokion (continued):*

Sin working corruption of old in the human race, O Virgin, didst thou dry up as a restless fire, having given birth unto Christ: therefore dry up the torrents of passions of my soul by thy prayers.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou Who broughtest all things from non-being, being created by the Word and perfected by the Spirit, O Supreme Almighty Ruler: establish me in Thy love!

*Troparion:*

By fiery Godhead is the Master and Lord, thy Son, named, O Pure One: therefore deliver me from passionate bondages, O Sovereign Lady, by thy prayers.

*Troparion:*

From the tempest of carnal passions deliver me thy servant swiftly, O Most Pure One, who didst give birth unto the Calm of salvation and the Fountain of dispassion.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Thou didst become a radiant lampstand of the Never-Setting Light, O Most Pure One, and didst enlighten the world: therefore by thy rays disperse the cloud of my soul.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Revive me who am dead in soul from the multitude of evil passions, O Sovereign Lady, who ineffably didst give birth unto Christ the Giver of life.

[f. 60rA] *Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Thou hast placed Thy steadfast love toward us, O Lord, for Thou didst deliver Thine Only-Begotten Son unto death for our sake: therefore we cry unto Thee thankfully: glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*

The warfare of carnal mind has come upon me and takes captive my soul: but O thou who didst give birth unto the Peace surpassing all understanding, grant me thy peace, O Sovereign Lady.

*Troparion:*

Thou didst pour forth unto the world intelligible myrrh from thy womb: therefore free my soul from defilements and passions, and grant me, O Most Pure One, the myrrh of dispassion through thy prayers.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Knowing thee manifestly as the Golden Jar that carried the Manna of life, the God-Man Christ, I pray: nourish my soul that faints with hunger, and refresh it with the stream of the Spirit.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Eve indeed introduced death of old through intemperance; but through thy pure virginity thou hast brought back the true Life: therefore deliver me from the death of sin through thy prayers.

[f. 60vA] *Ode 5. Heirmos:*

He Who is invisible appeared upon earth, and He Who is incomprehensible lived voluntarily with men, and rising early unto Thee, we sing Thy praise, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*

Having become our Redemption, thy Son, O Virgin, delivered all from corruption: therefore deliver me from carnal mind, and from the assault of passions.

*Troparion:*

With the fire of thy prayers, O Theotokos, burn up the thorns of passionate thoughts: thou who with the tongs of the live coal didst cleanse mankind.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Thou didst become the Heifer of God, [who didst bear] for our sake the divine Bullock sacrificed out of love for mankind: by Whose divine Blood, cleanse my heart.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Of old I acquired the comely garment of virtues, whereof I stripped myself through my slothfulness; but do thou, O Virgin, now clothe me in brightness by thy prayers.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

The uttermost abyss of sins has surrounded me, and my spirit fails; but stretching forth Thy high arm, O Master, save me, O Helmsman, even as Peter.

*Troparion:*

The grievous craftiness of malice has shaken the temple of my soul; but having given birth unto Christ Who established the earth upon the waters, establish [me] by thy prayers.

[f. 61rA] *Troparion:*

Thou didst loose the debt of earthborn mortals, having given birth unto the Pearl of great price: therefore, O Most Pure One, swiftly loose the bonds of my transgressions, and of passions and afflictions.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

God the all-good, having been born from thy womb, has given thee as a refuge and divine protection unto the faithful: therefore, O Most Pure One, cover me also with thy hands, and preserve me.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Being harmfully engulfed often by the waves of life, I, wretched one, and ever tempest-tossed by the enslavements of the enemy, now cry unto thee, O Theotokos: help me.

*Lord, have mercy (3 times). Glory... Both now...*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3.*

*Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Боже́ственныя вѣ́ры):* [^36]

Thou didst become the divine Tabernacle of the Word, O only Most Pure Virgin Mother, who in purity didst surpass the Angels: cleanse me, who am defiled above all men by carnal transgressions, with divine waters through thy prayers, granting great mercy, O Pure One.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

Thou Who of old didst bedew the three pious youths in the Chaldean flame, illuminate us also with the radiant fire of Godhead, as we cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

[f. 61vA] *Troparion:*

Assemblies of crafty demons have surrounded me, and carnal multitudes of passions; but having given birth unto Christ Who is mighty in battles, deliver me from frequent sorrows and impending assaults.

*Troparion:*

The One Who is unapproachable in essence to the Cherubim, and fearful to the angelic ranks, dwelt in thee, O Pure One, and renewed us: through Whom show me, O Sovereign Lady, to be fearful unto the invisible enemies.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

A spiritual paradise didst thou become, O Virgin, which put forth the Tree of life: whereof Adam having partaken was delivered from the mortal fruit: therefore sweeten me now also, and deliver me from the taste of passions.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Deliver [me] from the mire of bodily passions and demonic temptations, O thou who didst give birth unto the Redeemer of mankind, the highest dwelling and consecrated temple of the God of our fathers.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Gathered together in the intolerable fire, standing before [it], the pious youths, unharmed by the flame, sang the divine hymn: Bless the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

[f. 62rA] *Troparion:*

O Cloud of light, [who didst bear] Him Who made the great luminaries in the world, both day and night: disperse every darkness of all my passions and transgressions by thy rays, and show me to be a son of the light, O Mother of God.

*Troparion:*

By the Rain that came down from heaven into thy womb, O Theotokos, enlighten and bedew my soul and my heart, and quench the flame of passions and afflictions, that I may fervently glorify thee unto all ages.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Without corruption, O Pure One, thou didst give birth unto the Wisdom of God, Who united into one flesh all things from non-being, both heavenly and earthly nature: by Whose word grant me wisdom and understanding, that I may now sing of thy divine Childbearing.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

May my soul and body be bedewed by thy prayers, O Sovereign Lady of the world, and swiftly deliver me from the spears and lightning, the diseases of sins, and the malice of passions: for thou art healing unto all men.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Upon Mount Sinai Moses beheld thee in the bush unburned, having conceived in thy womb the divine Fire; and Daniel beheld thee as the unhewn Mountain; and Isaiah cried: A Rod budded forth from the root of David!

[f. 62vA] *Troparion:*

Thou didst appear as a loom divinely woven for Christ, from which the Spirit prepared the garment of most divine-princely flesh: clothe me therefore in the garment of chastity, O Pure One, by thy prayers.

*Troparion:*

O Spiritual Vineyard, who didst bud forth for us the Divine Cluster, wherefrom we are watered with the water of incorruption: pour forth unto my soul wine of compunction and cleansing, by thy prayers.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

O pure and holy Bridal Chamber, through whom God in nature was betrothed unto the nature of men, O Mother: unite me unto thy Son and make me His own in divine life, by thy prayers.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Having given birth unto Christ the Fountain of incorruption, O Virgin, thou didst transform the pasture of corruption of human nature: put to death therefore in me the scorching and tempestuous rush of passions, by thy prayers.

*Then, It is truly meet: Trisagion: and after the Our Father: and the rest as usual. And the Dismissal.*

*(Woodcut: Vignette / Cartouche with double-headed eagle emblem)*

[f. 63rA] *(Woodcut: Decorative headpiece with floral foliage)*

## ON MONDAY AT SMALL COMPLINE (TONE 3)

*On Monday at Compline,*[^37]

*Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*Ode 1, Tone 3. Heirmos:*

He Who of old by divine beckoning gathered the waters into one gathering, and divided the sea for the people of Israel: this is our God, exceedingly glorified; unto Him alone let us sing, for He has been glorified.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Having spent my life in slothfulness, I have drawn near unto the end of my days; but do thou thyself, O Most Pure One, remember me in the final time, and grant me compunction as I pray, that I may weep bitterly over my boundless transgressions.

*Troparion:*

Having foolishly defiled the beauty made after the image of God by the passions of my flesh, O Virgin, I was terrified of God's indignation and of the dreadful threat of fire; unto thee have I fled, O tenderhearted Mother: have mercy on me.

*Glory...*

[f. 63vA] *Troparion:*

Bending my knees, I, wretched one, ask for thy help, O Most Holy Virgin; do thou give ear unto my ailing soul, and disperse the impending cloud of my sorrow with the ray of thy prayers.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Grant the passionate furrows of my soul to be richly watered with tears, and vouchsafe me, O Sovereign Lady, to bear fruit a hundredfold, and fill my heart with every joy, that I may glorify thee.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou Who didst bring all things from non-existence, which are built by the Word and accomplished by the Spirit, O Almighty Most High, establish me in Thy love.

*Troparion:*

Grant me a rain-cloud of tears, O Good Theotokos, wherewith quench the furnace of my passions, and wash away from me every defilement of soul.

*Troparion:*

In iniquities I have defiled the nobility of my soul, O Most Pure One, and I tremble at the inquiry, when the Word shall examine the dignity of my image.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

The tempest of transgressions having now seized me has brought me down into the depth of despair: but stretch forth thy hand unto me, O Pure One, and guide me unto repentance.

*Both now...*

[f. 64rA] *Theotokion:*

Deliver thy servant from gehenna and every other threat in the hour of judgment, O All-Immaculate One, and make [me] a partaker of the Kingdom of thy Son and God.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Thou hast placed Thy steadfast love toward us, O Lord, for Thou didst deliver Thine Only-Begotten Son unto death for our sake: therefore we cry unto Thee thankfully: glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*

The wounds of shameful deeds have reached even unto my wretched soul, O Pure One; therefore held fast by thoughts of clay, I cry aloud with pain: despise me not, O Sovereign Lady, thy servant.

*Troparion:*

Spiritual beasts have now unmercifully surrounded me, striving unsparingly to snatch away my humble soul, O All-Immaculate One; but by thy Son, O Most Pure One, crush their soul-corrupting jaws.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Be thou an intercession unto thy servant, O Sovereign Lady, we pray, and from the expected threat snatch thy servants, O Pure One, that we may cry unto thee giving thanks: Glory to thee, Queen of all!

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

When the Bridegroom shall come by night to judge the earth: then vouchsafe me, O Most Pure One, to go forth to meet Him with a shining lamp, and to be ready for His coming.

[f. 64vA] *Ode 5. Heirmos:*

He Who is invisible appeared upon earth, and He Who is incomprehensible lived voluntarily with men, and rising early unto Thee, we sing Thy praise, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*

Many dogs have truly surrounded me; an assembly of wicked spirits has encompassed me; but do thou, O Most Pure One, now destroy their counsels.

*Troparion:*

The wicked one has now dug a pit for me, and strives to cast me therein; but by thy right hand, O Sovereign Lady, let him fall into the pit that he made.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Let me not be rebuked by the fury of thy Son at the time of His Coming, nor chastised by His wrath, O All-Hymned One: do thou save me by thy prayers.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Behold my weakness, O Pure One; behold the humiliation of my wretched soul, and the uprising of incorporeal enemies, and deliver me from their harm.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

The uttermost abyss of sins has surrounded me, and my spirit fails; but stretching forth Thy high arm, O Master, save me, O Helmsman, even as Peter.

*Troparion:*

By the beckoning of the Creator of all, when my soul is about to part from the flesh, O All-Immaculate One, set me free from the bonds of those who hate me, O All-Hymned Theotokos.

[f. 65rA] *Troparion:*

Dry up the turbid rivers of my wicked deeds, O Mother of God, with streams of compunction, and guide me unto the water of relief in the Day of Judgment.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Thou knowest the weakness of my soul, and the infirmity of my mind, O Most Pure One, and the impotence of my flesh: therefore save thy servant, for I have acquired thee as an unconquerable Protectress.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Grant me a stream of spiritual tears, O Maiden, wherewith I may wash away the mire of my transgressions, and the tumult of passions, O All-Immaculate One, and bodily defilements.

*Lord, have mercy (3 times). Glory... Both now...*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3.*

*Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):* [^38]

O thou who didst carry the Merciful One in thy womb: have compassion upon him that flees beneath thy protection, and asks from his soul for thy divine help, O Sovereign Lady, and vouchsafe him mercy; when we shall stand before all creation, deliver [us], O Pure One, from eternal fire and every condemnation.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

Enduring the molten body [of the idol] made through Persian deceit, the three children bowed not down, singing in the midst of the fire: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

[f. 65vA] *Troparion:*

The wounds of transgressions have poured out upon me even unto my soul, O Pure One, and a wretched ruin has surrounded me: but snatch me from this distress.

*Troparion:*

Sprinkle me, O Pure One, with the Blood from the ribs of thy Child, according to the multitude of thy [mercies], O Mother, and wash me with streams of tears, and cleanse me from every defilement.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Grant contrition unto my soul and humility unto my heart, O All-Pure One, that I may be delivered from the oppressive craftiness of those who continually and unsparingly hunt after me.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

As the merciful Mother of God, O Sovereign Lady, vouchsafe mercy also unto thy servants who cry aloud with faith unto thy Son: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Gathered together in the intolerable fire, standing before [it], the pious youths, unharmed by the flame, sang the divine hymn: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*

Crush the arrows of the archer, O Pure One, by the dominion of thy Son, and let his iniquity come down upon his own crown, that I may sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*

Illuminate my darkened heart with thy light, O Maiden, and with the armor of light open unto me the gates of light, as I cry:

[f. 66rA] Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and praise and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Having fallen asleep unto death, I lie in the tomb of despair; but do thou thyself raise me up, O Virgin, and vouchsafe me to sing vigilantly: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, praise and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Cease not, O Pure One, praying for those who honor thee in compunction, that they may be delivered from the snares of the devil, crying unto thy Son: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, praise and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

In the shadowy writing of the Law we believers perceive a figure: for every male child that opens the womb is holy to God. Therefore the First-born Word, the Son of the Unoriginate Father, first-born from an unwedded Mother, do we magnify.

*Troparion:*

The surge of passions tempest-tosses my soul, and the turmoil of foul thoughts, and the storm of wicked men continually troubles me; but as thou art the lover of mankind, O Virgin, swiftly deliver me from the distress of those who hold me fast.

*Troparion:*

O humble soul, depart from thy wicked deeds, and cease from malice, and [f. 66vA] anger not God, but receive His commandments diligently: for thou hast the Mother of God directing thy ways.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

As thou didst bear the Lord of all, free me from passions and grievous sins, and enrich me with good deeds according to thy great tenderheartedness: that rejoicing, I may magnify thee with love, O All-Immaculate One.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

The end draws near, O my soul; the judgment seat is at the doors; depart from shameful deeds, and take hold of a good life: for thou hast the Theotokos as a Champion, delivering thee from every affliction.

*Then, It is truly meet: Trisagion: and after the Our Father: Troparia, and the rest as usual. And the Dismissal.*

*(Woodcut: The Annunciation — Archangel Gabriel appearing to the Blessed Virgin Mary)*

[f. 67rA] *(Woodcut: Decorative headpiece with foliage and stars)*

## ON TUESDAY AT SMALL COMPLINE (TONE 3)

*On Tuesday at Compline,*[^39]

*Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*Ode 1, Tone 3. Heirmos:*

He Who of old by divine beckoning gathered the waters into one gathering, and divided the sea for the people of Israel: this is our God, exceedingly glorified; unto Him alone let us sing, for He has been glorified.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Knowing thee, the Mother of God, to be the fountain of tenderheartedness and mercy, I approach and pray unto thy goodness to grant me compunction, that I may lament and weep over my transgressions, O All-Pure Sovereign Lady.

*Troparion:*

Grant me a drop of spiritual tears, washing away all the defilement of my deeds and evil thoughts, cleansing the foulness of my soul, and making me a temple of the Spirit of God.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Tempest-tossed by the tumult and surge of my transgressions, and engulfed continually and fiercely by the slaveries that war against me, and now cast down into the depth of perdition, I cry unto thee, O All-Immaculate One: save me!

*Both now...*

[f. 67vA] *Theotokion:*

For the sake of tenderheartedness, O Mother and Sovereign Lady of all, have mercy upon my wretched soul, and deliver me from eternal fire and from demonic assault, as I now flee beneath thy protection, O Theotokos.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou Who didst bring all things from non-existence, which are built by the Word and accomplished by the Spirit, O Almighty Most High, establish me in Thy love.

*Troparion:*

Illuminate my mind darkened by evil thoughts with divine light, O All-Hymned One: for thou didst bear the Light, the Ever-Existing One Who shone forth from the Father.

*Troparion:*

Having tripped the steps of my soul, the ancient enemy drags me down to the earth; but by thy right hand, O Pure Sovereign Lady, raise me up.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

With the cries of the Publican, I, wretched one, cry unto thee: cleanse me, O Sovereign Lady, and grant remission of transgressions unto thy servant by thy prayer.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Heal the wounds of my soul, and calm the troubled surge of thoughts, O Sovereign Lady, and grant me a peaceful disposition.

[f. 68rA] *Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Thou hast placed Thy steadfast love toward us, O Lord, for Thou didst deliver Thine Only-Begotten Son unto death for our sake: therefore we cry unto Thee thankfully: glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*

I have defiled my flesh with pleasures and voluptuousness, and muddied the purity of my soul with foul thoughts, and darkened my mind: despise not therefore thy servant, O Sovereign Lady.

*Troparion:*

Be thou my healing, and refuge, and strength, and a Horn of salvation, O Pure One, and protectress: delivering me ever from every sorrow, and putting all my enemies to shame.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Held fast now by many temptations, wretched one, and ever submerged by demonic crafts, I now flee unto thee, O Sovereign Lady: calm the storm by thy gentle beckoning, and save me thy servant.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

The ignorant night of passions holds me fast, wretched one; but by thy light, O Good One, disperse the clouds of my soul, and guide me unto the light of the commandments of God, O All-Immaculate One.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

As Isaiah figuratively beheld God upon an exalted throne, borne on high by the angels of glory, he cried: Woe is me, wretched one!

[f. 68vA] For I have foreseen God incarnate, the Never-Setting Light, and Lord of peace.

*Troparion:*

The time of my life has vanished like smoke, and I have drawn near unto the gates of death, and I dread demonic assault: for I have always done their works. But do thou have compassion, O All-Immaculate One, and save me.

*Troparion:*

Drying up the abyss of my evils, O Virgin, grant me rivers of tears, and quench the entire flame of my passions, O Maiden, and vouchsafe me to be delivered from the fire and from the other torments on the Day of Judgment.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Heal my soul which is sick with sins, O Most Pure One, by thy mercies, and vouchsafe me ever to walk in humility in the commandments of thy Son, that I may receive His grace.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Bereaved of bridal garments, O my Son, is the divine Church, yet clothed in the holy Blood from Thy side; but I, looking upon Thy sufferings upon the Cross, am full of pain: cried the Mother of the Word weeping.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

The uttermost abyss of sins has surrounded me, and my spirit fails; but stretching forth Thy high arm, O Master, save me, O Helmsman, even as Peter.

[f. 69rA] *Troparion:*

The Son of God showed thee to be a great refuge unto sinners, having become incarnate, O Bride of God, from thy pure blood: therefore let thy prayer be protection unto thy servants.

*Troparion:*

Enlighten the eyes of my heart darkened by unworthy thoughts with thy radiance, O Good One, and make me a son of the light, and settle me in a place of light.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Waves of passionate thoughts continually trouble me, O Most Pure One, and the tempest of evil spirits submerges me: but establish me upon the rock of dispassion.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

I have fallen asleep in the death of soul, and lie in the grave of despair: but stretch forth thy hand unto me, and raise me up as I pray, guiding me unto life through repentance.

*Then, Lord, have mercy (3 times). Glory... Both now...*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*[^40]

Having acquired the Cross of thy Son as a rod of strength, O Theotokos: thereby we cast down the insolence of the enemy, who ever magnify thee with love.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

Enduring the golden image made through Persian deceit, the three children bowed not down, singing in the midst of the furnace of fire: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

[f. 69vA] Gird my soul with strength, O Pure One, which is enfeebled by sins, and save me as I cry unto thy Son: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*

I dread the hour of death because of the immortal and eternal torment; unto thee do I fall down: save me, O Maiden, from the snare of those who hunt after me.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

O thou who didst contain in thy womb the Incomprehensible God: free my mind, which is constricted by many transgressions, from their condemnation, O Theotokos.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

The race of men prays unto thee, O Theotokos: have mercy, O Sovereign Lady, upon thy servants who cry aloud with faith unto thy Son: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Gathered together in the intolerable fire, the youths standing for piety, unharmed by the flame, sang the divine hymn: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*

I have truly followed the Prodigal, and have spent all my life in sins; now I cry: I have sinned against Thee; make me, O Maiden, as one of the hired servants of thy Son and Creator, that I may glorify thee unto all ages.

[f. 70rA] *Troparion:*

My soul is filled with evils, and I have been numbered with all that go down into the pit; but do thou, O Theotokos Virgin, lead me forth from the wounds of passions and from the mire of my evils.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Implore Christ, O Theotokos, having borne Him from thy pure womb: to grant me forgiveness of many transgressions, that I may sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and praise and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

When by the command of God my soul is about to depart from this life, snatch me from the snares of those who hunt after me, O Most Pure One, as I cry unto thee: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and praise and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

In the shadowy writing of the Law we believers perceive a figure: for every male child that opens the womb is holy to God. Therefore the First-born Word of the Unoriginate Father, the Son first-born of an unwedded Mother, do we magnify.

*Troparion:*

The race of men, O All-Immaculate One, having now been enriched by thine unconquerable prayer, cries day and night: Cease not ever praying unto the Creator and thy Son, to have compassion upon those who sing thy praise.

[f. 70vA] *Troparion:*

The arrows of unseemly passions have wounded my soul through demonic assaults, and with opposing arrows of thoughts they ever confound my mind. Therefore heal for me, O Maiden, these incurable wounds.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Repress swiftly from me, O Pure Bride of God, the wounds of warring enemies: for unable to endure any longer their uprising and much craftiness and impudence, wretched one: hasten, O Sovereign Lady, to deliver me from them.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Thou knowest the weakness of my humble body, the wounds of my soul, the sighing of my heart, and the wandering of my mind, and delusion: therefore by thy tenderheartedness grant healing unto both.

*Then, It is truly meet: and the usual Dismissal.*

*(Woodcut: Jacob's Ladder — The Virgin and Child enthroned at the summit of the ladder between the sun, moon, and stars, with the Patriarch Jacob sleeping at the foot)*

[f. 71rA] *(Woodcut: Decorative headpiece band)*

## ON WEDNESDAY AT SMALL COMPLINE (TONE 3)

*On Wednesday at Compline,*[^41]

*Canon of Supplication to the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*Ode 1, Tone 3. Heirmos:*

He Who of old by divine beckoning gathered the waters into one gathering, and divided the sea for the people of Israel: this is our God, exceedingly glorified; unto Him alone let us sing, for He has been glorified.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Grant me contrite sighing, O Theotokos, and a tear of the soul: that I may weep for my many transgressions and the uncorrected state of my conscience, O Maiden, and may implore thy tenderheartedness.

*Troparion:*

Stumbling in thoughts and falling into the abyss of perdition, I pray for thy protection, O Virgin: establish immovably the weakness of my mind upon the firm rock of God's commandments.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

I bear the barren fig tree [of fruitlessness],[^42] I fear sudden cutting down: lest my Creator God send me into the fire. Anticipate [it], O Sovereign Lady, and make me fruitful with works of repentance, that I may glorify thee.

*Both now...*

[f. 71vA] *Theotokion:*

As an undefiled Temple of the King, cleanse, O Most Pure One, my heart defiled with unseemly passions, by thy mercy: and enrich [it] with chastity and pure living, and with deeds, O Full of the Grace of God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou Who didst bring all things from non-existence, which are built by the Word and accomplished by the Spirit, O Almighty Most High, establish me in Thy love.

*Troparion:*

Bitter and many are those who afflict me, and there is none to find deliverance from those that have surrounded me: but despise not, O Pure One, my supplication.

*Troparion:*

Weighed down by a multitude of evils and by my many transgressions, I cannot look up unto heavenly height: but do thou grant me remission, O Most Pure One.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

The crafty serpent strives daily to tear in pieces my humble soul: but crush, O Theotokos, his soul-capturing fangs.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Deliver me, O Theotokos, from every impending calamity and from the sinful storm, thy servant, and guide me unto the haven of salvation by thy prayers.

[f. 72rA] *Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Thou hast placed Thy steadfast love toward us, O Lord, for Thou didst deliver Thine Only-Begotten Son unto death for our sake: therefore we cry unto Thee thankfully: glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*

I tremble as I contemplate the abyss of my sins and the fierce and great tempest of opposing thoughts: but do thou guide the helm, and direct me unto calm, O Most Pure One.

*Troparion:*

Grievous perplexity has now overtaken my soul, lack of understanding and forgetfulness of the evils which I have done in life: but do thou, O Most Pure One, lead me unto repentance for the things done by me.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Quench the fire of passions with streams of tears, and burn up worldly care that removes my mind afar off, and the thorns [of vanity], O Mother of God, who didst bear the Fire of the Spirit: for thou art my protection and praise.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

By the wave of temptations and tempests I am wholly submerged through crafty thoughts, and continually dragged down into the depth of despair; I cry unto thee: O Sovereign Lady, save me thy servant!

[f. 72vA] *Ode 5. Heirmos:*

As Isaiah figuratively beheld God upon an exalted throne, borne on high by the angels of glory, he cried: Woe is me, wretched one! For I have foreseen God incarnate, the Never-Setting Light, and Lord of peace.

*Troparion:*

Thou knowest the illness of my soul, O Virgin, and the weakness of my flesh, and the corruption and instability of my mind. Therefore vouchsafe me thy mercy and goodness, that I may glorify thee, being saved by thy protection.

*Troparion:*

Shut not against me the tenderheartedness of thy compassions, O All-Immaculate Sovereign Lady: but unto him that approaches thee in compunction, grant thy mercies, and relief from grievous evils, and forgiveness: that I may thankfully sing, magnifying thee.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Those who hate me and afflict me exceedingly have multiplied against me, O Pure Virgin, more than the hairs of my head, seeking to tear me in pieces and to make [me] food for themselves: do thou fill them with shame and turn them back.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

O Virgin, who didst give peace unto the world and salvation unto all, having brought forth Divine Peace: appease the war of passions of my soul and body, O Pure One, through the fear and love of Christ the Savior.

[f. 73rA] *Ode 6. Heirmos:*

The uttermost abyss of sins has surrounded me, and my spirit fails; but stretching forth Thy high arm, O Master, save me, O Helmsman, even as Peter.

*Troparion:*

In thoughts, and words, and deeds, there is none that has lived so evilly upon earth as I. Therefore I ask for thy love toward mankind, O Sovereign Lady, that I may obtain mercy.

*Troparion:*

The seekings of evil transgressions continually bring forth sorrows. Therefore we are now held fast by bitter temptations: help us, who art the only salvation of Christians.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Thou givest birth, and art shown to be Pure after Childbirth, O Virgin Theotokos. Therefore pray unceasingly unto thy Offspring, that we may be delivered from every harm of the enemy by thy prayers.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

By the prayers of Thine All-Pure Mother, and of all the Prophets and Martyrs, and of the Holy Disciples, O Word of God, grant us peace, and cleansing of transgressions.

*Lord, have mercy (3 times). Glory... Both now...*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*[^43]

Behold, my life has passed away in great negligence, O Most Pure One, and now I have drawn near unto the time of my departure, and [f. 73vA] I am terrified by my enemies, lest they tear my soul in pieces, O All-Immaculate One, and cast [it] down into the depth of perdition; but have compassion upon thy servant, O Virgin, and deliver me from their affliction.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

As of old Thou didst bedew the three pious youths in the Chaldean flame, with the radiant fire of Godhead illuminate us also, as we cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*

Thee, the Pure and Undefiled Mother of God, I, defiled in body and soul and wretched through unclean deeds, implore, O Sovereign Lady, hoping in thy mercy: have compassion upon me, O Most Pure One.

*Troparion:*

The multitude of my transgressions and the trial of evils utterly fills me with perplexity, and drags me into the depth of despair; but do thou, O Most Pure Sovereign Lady, save me who am perishing and grievously drowning.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

According to the multitude of the compassions of Thy goodness, overlook the multitude of our sins, O Good One, and be well-disposed unto those that cry unceasingly: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

From the burden of passions, and the depth of sorrows, and worldly temptations, draw us, O Blessed and Pure One, unto [f. 74rA] dispassion and joyful gladness, who cry: Thou alone art full of the grace of God!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Having quenched the flame of material fire with the immaterial, the divine youths sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, praise and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*

The bitterness of pleasures, having passed through all bodily senses, unseemly defiles my soul and drags [it] unto death; O Sovereign Lady of the world, be thou my salvation.

*Troparion:*

Upon thee have I cast my soul and heart and body, having no other hope except thee, O Sovereign Lady. Therefore grant me thy rich mercy and grace.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

The serpent, having crawled up with poison through carnal pleasure, has grievously put my wretched soul to death; but revive [it], O Pure One, by countervailing remedies, through thy prayers unto thy Son.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

O thou who didst bear the King and Savior, the Prince of Peace: implore Him to send peace swiftly unto the world, that in peace we may give glory unto Him.

[f. 74vA] *Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Thee, the unburnt bush, and holy Virgin, Mother of the Light, Theotokos, the hope of us all, do we magnify.

*Troparion:*

Cleanse, O Pure One, the defilement of passionate thoughts from my mind, clothing me in the radiant garment of dispassion.

*Troparion:*

Open unto me, O Virgin, the divine entrances of repentance, blocking and barring the entrances of my passions and pleasures by thy power.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Hear the voice of my sighing and the voice of my weeping, and grant cleansing and salvation unto my wretched soul, O All-Immaculate Virgin.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Daniel the Prophet foresaw thy Childbirth, O Virgin; he foresaw [it] clearly in truth upon the Throne; he foresaw Him Whom he proclaimed as the Ancient of Days.

*Then, It is truly meet: Trisagion, and the rest as usual: and the Dismissal.*

*(Woodcut: The Ascension of the Lord — Christ ascending in glory within an oval mandorla supported by angels, with the Theotokos and Apostles below looking up)*

[f. 75rA] *(Woodcut: Decorative headpiece band)*

## ON THURSDAY AT SMALL COMPLINE (TONE 3)

*On Thursday at Compline,*[^44]

*Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*Ode 1, Tone 3. Heirmos:*

He Who of old by divine beckoning gathered the waters into one gathering, and divided the sea for the people of Israel: this is our God, exceedingly glorified; unto Him alone let us sing, for He has been glorified.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Thee, the Shadow-Covered Mountain, which Habakkuk the Prophet foresaw by the Spirit, I implore, O Most Pure One: overshadow me who am scorched by passions, and deliver me from the deathly shade of grievous calamities.

*Troparion:*

By the sprinkling of the Divine Blood shed from the Divine Rib of thy Son, O Pure One, wash away the wounds of my heart: that I may magnify thee, and glorify thee as is meet, who art ever-blessed and all-immaculate.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Equal in working unto the Father, thou didst bring forth the Word, Who renewed the nature of men: implore Him [f. 75vA] to vouchsafe divine consolation unto me, perplexed and enfeebled by the enemy's crafts, O Pure One.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Grant me cleansing of transgressions, O Virgin, by whatsoever divine prayers of thine: for thou hast powerful prayer, O Sovereign Lady; and deliver those who sing thy praise from transgressions, and passions, and sorrows, and tribulations.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou Who didst bring all things from non-existence, which are built by the Word and accomplished by the Spirit, O Almighty Most High, establish me in Thy love.

*Troparion:*

As thou art the Mother of the ineffable God, rich in compassions, being an intercession, O Most Pure One, implore [Him] to have compassion upon us who are afflicted.

*Troparion:*

As thou art the universal Temple of the Creator, implore the Comforter to dwell in me, who have become a den of soul-corrupting robbers, O Pure Virgin.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

As thou didst hold in thine arms Him Who by His divine beckoning upholds all things, O Theotokos, look down upon me and deliver me from improper beholding unto passions.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

I implore thy tender mercies, open [them] unto me, O Most Pure Theotokos, and appear unto me as a fervent Helper and salvation in temptations.

[f. 76rA] *Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Thou hast placed Thy steadfast love toward us, O Lord, for Thou didst deliver Thine Only-Begotten Son unto death for our sake. Therefore we cry unto Thee thankfully: glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*

O Christ, Who voluntarily didst endure wounds and slaying as God: heal my soul, wounded by the robbers of demonic afflictions, by the prayers of her who bore Thee, O Thou Who alone art Plenteous in mercy!

*Troparion:*

I am the work of Thy hands, O Creator, and Thy creation: but the malice of the serpent has crushed me through the pleasures of life. Therefore, O Christ, renew me by the entreaties of her who bore Thee, O Word!

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Thou didst bring forth beyond word the Word of the Father, releasing men from every unreason: implore Him diligently, O Virgin alone ever-pure, to set me free who am enslaved by irrational pleasures.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Consecrated by tabernacles, being wholly full of light, thou ever pourest forth healing unto us from thy palm, gushing fragrant myrrh unto all, O All-Immaculate Bride of God!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

As Isaiah figuratively beheld God upon an exalted throne, borne on high by the angels of glory, he cried: Woe is me, wretched one! [f. 76vA] for I have foreseen God incarnate, the Never-Setting Light, and Lord of peace.

*Troparion:*

Transform the illnesses of my flesh and the sorrow of my soul, and drive away the clouds of despondency, O Virgin, cloud of light, and grant health and relief from sicknesses unto me who sing and glorify thee with love.

*Troparion:*

Thee do I now offer as Mediatress and Suppliant unto Him Who was born of thee, being full of every sin: be thou the correction of my life and the Guide unto the path of divine understanding.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Cleanse my mind, O Virgin, and enlighten my soul, and make me a partaker of divine glory: for behold, I am filled with evils, and enslaved to material things through pleasures, and bring an impure conscience.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

As thou art the Divine Vine, O Virgin, who didst bud forth the beautiful Cluster that gives unto souls the drink of immortality, O Most Holy Maiden: do thou give me to drink of His sweetness, and take away the drunkenness of pleasures, and save me.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

O Compassionate One Who didst come at the end of the ages, unto me who am about to sink in tempestuous temptations, crying unceasingly: save me, O Savior, even as Thou didst save the Prophet from the beast!

[f. 77rA] *Troparion:*

I have surpassed the Pharisee lifted up in mind, being ever exalted, and have joined myself unto the pits of measureless transgressions, being grievously brought low; O thou who alone art Pure, deliver me and have compassion upon me.

*Troparion:*

Having a marvelous Conception and Childbirth, make thy mercies marvelous upon me, wretched one, O Theotokos: for I was conceived in iniquities, and born, and enslaved to pleasures.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

I weep, and lament, and sigh, when I remember the Dread Judgment Seat: for I have acquired evil deeds; but do thou, O Unwedded Virgin, Mother of God, stand by me in the dread hour.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

No mind can understand or speak, O Pure Virgin, of the strange and most glorious wonder wrought upon thee: how thou didst give birth and remainest again Pure: for He that was born is God by essence.

*Then, Lord, have mercy (3 times). Glory... Both now...*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*[^45]

Thine Unwedded and Pure Mother, O Christ, beholding Thee dead and hanging upon the Tree, weeping as a mother, said: What has the lawless and ungrateful assembly of the Jews rendered unto Thee, which enjoyed Thy many gifts, O my Son? I praise Thy divine condescension.

[f. 77vA] *Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The three youths in the furnace, prefiguring the Trinity, trod down the fiery threat, and praising sang: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*

There is no salvation for me from works, O Sovereign Lady, for I add sins unto sins, and malice unto malice; anticipate therefore by thy prayer, O Pure One, and save me.

*Troparion:*

Judgment is at the doors, and the tribunal is ready; make ready, O humble soul, and cry aloud: When Thou judgest me, O Word, condemn me not, through the prayers of her that bore Thee!

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Burned by the fire of sin, I have died, and I offer a barren soul, and cry unto thee, O Most Pure One: make me fruitful, O thou who by thy Fruit didst destroy corruption!

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

O strange mystery! O marvelous understanding! How God appeared upon earth as man; as He knows, as He wills, as He is well-pleased, and works even as He wills.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Gathered together in the unstable fire, the youths standing for piety, unharmed by the flame, sang the divine hymn: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

[f. 78rA] *Troparion:*

Being wholly fair, as the companion of the Churches of all, O Theotokos: fill me with good deeds, who have lived in malice and ended all my life in negligence: that I may glorify thee unto all ages.

*Troparion:*

Even as Thou didst most gloriously deliver of old the Prophet from the belly of the whale, O Word of God: so deliver now my soul, O Savior, that has slipped into the depth of perdition, having the Virgin who unwedded bore Thee praying unto Thee.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Having found me clothed in a beautiful garment, the workers of malice stripped me thereof; but do thou thyself, O Virgin Mother of God, clothe me in divine garments of repentance, through thy prayers.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Him before Whom every creature trembles, Him didst thou hold in thine arms, O Pure One, Who through tenderheartedness appeared sitting as an Infant: implore Him to save all that cry in faith: We exalt thee, O Most Pure One, unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

In the shadowy writing of the Law we believers perceive a figure: for every male child that opens the womb is holy to God. Therefore the First-born Word of the Unoriginate Father, the Son first-born from an unwedded Mother, do we magnify.

*Troparion:*

Consumed in mind and soul, and having defiled my conscience with malice, and shown myself naked of all good things, [f. 78vA] O Incorrupt and Undefiled Virgin, despise me not: but adorn [me] with the works of piety.

*Troparion:*

I am filled with evils, I am filled with thoughts alienating me from Thee, O Lover of mankind! Therefore I sigh and cry: Accept me repenting, and cast me not away, through the entreaties of her that bore Thee, O Plenteous in mercy Benefactor!

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

May I be delivered by thy prayers, O All-Immaculate Maiden, from all wrath, and death-bearing passions, and the fierce fire of gehenna, I, an unrighteous man, fleeing from evil enemies unto thy shelter and calling upon thee for help.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Having found thee fair in soul, fair in thoughts, and fair in body, the Most Fair One took flesh virginally from thy loins, as He knows, adorning our formless nature, O Virgin: implore Him that we may be saved.

*Then, It is truly meet: Trisagion, and after the Our Father: Troparia, and the rest as usual: and the Dismissal.*

*(Woodcut: Decorative floral vignette)*

[f. 79rA] *(Woodcut: Decorative headpiece with emblems and the Holy Cross)*

## ON FRIDAY AT SMALL COMPLINE (TONE 3)

*On Friday at Compline,*[^46]

*Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*Ode 1, Tone 3. Heirmos:*

He Who of old by divine beckoning gathered the waters into one gathering, and divided the sea for the people of Israel: this is our God, exceedingly glorified; unto Him alone let us sing, for He has been glorified.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Having brought forth the Author of salvation and the Giver of life beyond all understanding, thou didst loose the bond of the condemnation of Eve our Fore-mother. Therefore all creation blesses thee, O Pure Mother of God.

*Troparion:*

Snatch me, who am afflicted with grievous sicknesses in soul and body, O Most Holy Virgin, from the depth of sorrow, delivering me from all dire straits: for thou, O Most Pure One, art the fountain of tenderheartedness.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

I have stripped myself of the garment of gladness, falling into sorrow and infirmity, and have been grievously wounded by enemies; anticipate [them] and deliver me, O Sovereign Lady, from the delusion that holds me fast: for thou art my refuge and hope.

*Both now...*

[f. 79vA] *Theotokion:*

Having acquired thee as a steadfast Protectress, O Bride of God, I flee beneath thy protection, praying: Despise me not, thy servant, weighed down by many passions, infirmities, and sorrows: but heal me by thy prayers, O Mother of God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou Who didst bring all things from non-existence, which are built by the Word and accomplished by the Spirit, O Almighty Most High, establish me in Thy love.

*Troparion:*

I, wretched one, am in perplexity and painful sorrow, beholding death following after me. Therefore, O Most Pure One, save me by thy prayer!

*Troparion:*

Behold, having wasted my life in evil and profligate deeds, [my conscience] accuses me and submerges me in despair; O Pure One, save me!

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

The abyss of thy compassions, poured out everywhere, O Sovereign Lady, for the sake of goodness alone, anoints with oil and soothes all that ever sing thy praise.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Having taken [humanity] from the first-fruits of our nature, O Bride of God, Thy Son and Lord unites those that sing thy praise, through thy prayers.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Thou hast placed Thy steadfast love toward us, O Lord, for Thou didst deliver Thine Only-Begotten Son unto death for our sake. Therefore we cry unto Thee thankfully: glory to Thy power, O Lord!

[f. 80rA] *Troparion:*

Having placed all my hope upon thee, O Sovereign Lady, I pray and fall down from my soul: deliver [me] from death-bearing sicknesses, leading me up unto the life of salvation: as thou didst bear Life!

*Troparion:*

Heal me, O Pure One, by thy prayers, thy wretched servant, paralyzed by the appearance of grievous things: for thou didst bear the Physician, rich in mercy, our God.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Deliver me who have fallen into grievous passions, and grant me the garment of gladness through thy divine visitation: for thou, O Virgin, didst bud forth Joy that surpasses every mind.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Thou hast been shown forth as another Heaven, O Theotokos, having ineffably shone forth the Sun of righteousness upon earth, O Pure One, through Whom the Ever-Existing Light of divine knowledge has shone upon us.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Unto Thee I rise early, O Creator of all, Who art Peace surpassing every mind: for Thy commandments are light; guide me in them!

*Troparion:*

Things causing death and passions have drawn near because of my measureless sins, O Mother of God; I diligently pray that thou mayest be the deliverance from my many evils.

*Troparion:*

I stretch forth my feeble hands unto thee, O Pure One: for I, wretched one, am weakened by carnal things. But do thou [f. 80vA] heal me of my many wounds, granting healing.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

I have stripped off the garment of health, and have put on a sorrowful robe in sicknesses; I pray unto thee, O Sovereign Lady: clothe me in health.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

As the sacred beauty of Martyrs and Apostles, and the protection of the faithful, O Theotokos, we all worthily glorify thee.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Bring me up from corruption, O Lord my God, Jonah cried, and I cry unto Thee: deliver me, O Savior, from the depth of my many evils, and guide me unto the light of Thy commandments.

*Troparion:*

A multitude of corrupting sicknesses has fallen upon me from my measureless transgressions, and encompassed me with deadness; but do thou thyself, O Theotokos, deliver me from them, and snatch me from every distress.

*Troparion:*

Having been cast down into the depth of transgressions and sicknesses, and measureless sorrows, and being utterly submerged in them: do thou, O Mother of God, stretch forth thy hand and bring me up unto salvation.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

We know thee as a firm foundation, O Theotokos, and a mighty help in sorrows. Therefore, fleeing unto thy protection, I implore thee: deliver me from fierce calamities and from grievous sicknesses.

*Both now...*

[f. 81rA] *Theotokion:*

As the Furrow that sprouted the Divine Ear of Grain, whereby the hearts of the faithful are nourished by faith and noetic hunger has ceased, let us sing of the Ark of God, the Virgin Mother of God.

*Then, Lord, have mercy (3 times). Glory... Both now...*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*[^47]

Having become flesh from thy womb, unsearchable of eternal nature, yet God became man and remained [God], He Who after childbirth preserved thee as Virgin Mother all-immaculate, even as before childbirth, the only Lord: Him do thou diligently implore to grant us great mercy.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

Thou Who of old didst refresh the three pious youths in the Chaldean flame, with the light-bearing fire of Godhead illumine us also, who cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*

Have tenderhearted compassion upon me, O Virgin Bride of God, who have slipped into the depth of despair; I pray thee to be my calm, O Most Pure One: for thou art ever the Haven of salvation unto those in distress.

*Troparion:*

Ineffably beyond understanding thou didst bear Christ, the Wisdom and Power of God. Therefore, being mighty in prayer and generous, grant me, O Theotokos, Hope of the faithful, the saving waters of healing.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Having enriched the ends [of the earth] with thy Divine Childbirth by the sweetness of nourishment, free me from corrupting illness and bitter passions, and grant [me] salvation, O All-Glorious Mother of God.

*Both now...*

[f. 81vA] *Theotokion:*

He Who prepared thee as His mystical gate and a marvelous protectress unto those on earth, through thee, O Pure One, has granted unto men a door that leads all up unto eternal life, as King and God of all.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The Babylonian furnace did not burn the youths, nor did the divine fire consume their spirit; therefore with the children we cry in faith: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*

Having acquired an unsearchable abyss of transgressions, now am I tossed by waves, O Pure One, submerged in sicknesses. Therefore, stretching forth a hand of help unto me, bring me up from the depth of sicknesses, I pray.

*Troparion:*

Thou hast appeared, O Pure Virgin, lauded Theotokos, as an earthly Throne unto God, and even more as the Heavenly Door. Therefore open unto me the door of salvation, O Mother of God, and set me free from the darkness of passions.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

As a vine thou didst put forth the Untilled Cluster, O Pure One, that gushes forth wine making glad the souls of those that sing His praise in faith, O All-Immaculate One.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Thou who remained incorrupt after childbirth, O All-Immaculate One: by thy prayers, O Pure One, deliver from corruption thy servants who cry in faith with singleness of soul: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

[f. 82rA] *Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Moses saw thee in the bush upon Mount Sinai, who didst unburnt conceive the Divine Fire in thy womb; and Daniel saw thee as the Uncut Mountain, [and] the Budded Rod, cried Isaiah, from the root of David.

*Troparion:*

O Good Virgin, Joy and Refuge from the world, Haven of the faithful, and Deliverance from sorrows: do thou stand by me in the hour of death, and deliver me from the demons seeking to tear me apart.

*Troparion:*

Having conceived in thy womb the Creator and God, O Virgin Mother Pure, by the power abiding in thee cast down the ragings of demons, and exalt the horn of those that sing of thy divine dominion and faithfully venerate thine icon.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Having made our nature new, He Who took flesh of thee, O Pure Virgin, makes [it] new and renews [it] by thy maternal prayer. Therefore, as our radiant and vigilant Protectress and true Theotokos, we unceasingly magnify thee in hymns.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Thou art the joy of Angels, thou art the beauty of the Righteous, thou art the hope of the faithful, thou art our preservation, thou art the Bridge leading those who magnify thee in faith and love unto ageless life.

*Then, It is truly meet: Trisagion, and after the Our Father: and the rest as usual: and the Dismissal.*

[f. 82vA] *(Woodcut: Full-page engraving of the Mother of God holding the Christ Child within an ornate arch, with the Holy Spirit as a Dove above, an inscription cartouche below, and the Russian imperial double-headed eagle at the base)*

---

# TONE 4

[f. 83rA] *(Woodcut: Decorative headpiece showing the Mother of God and Christ Child within a medallion, surrounded by floral scrollwork and vines)*

## ON SATURDAY AT SMALL COMPLINE (TONE 4)

*On Saturday at Compline,*[^48]

*Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*Ode 1, Tone 4. Heirmos:*

Israel of old traversed the abyss of the Red Sea with unwetted feet; and by Moses' arms outstretched in the likeness of the Cross, he conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

O thou who alone protectest in temptations and sorrows, as thou art All-Good, accept from the heart the supplications of those that fervently flee unto thy protection, O Most Pure One.

*Troparion:*

Having found thee as a storm-free Haven, I, unreasoning one, driving away tempestuous and distressful assaults, sing unto thee a hymn of thanksgiving, O Mother unwedded.

*Glory...*

[f. 83vA] Beholding me with thy maternal and gentle eye, O Mother of God, held fast in tribulation and sorrow, swiftly set me free: for I call upon thee for help.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Unto me who am grievously bowed down by distressing sorrows, O Sovereign Lady: as the only merciful, Good Protectress of thy servants, stretch forth a hand of supplication, and deliver me from fierce calamities.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

The bow of the mighty has grown weak, and the feeble have girded themselves with strength; therefore my heart has been established in the Lord.

*Troparion:*

Having acquired thee as a strong weapon and a rampart, I conquer the hosts of adversaries, and sing of thy greatness, O Theotokos Unwedded.

*Troparion:*

Thou dost destroy the furnace of griefs and quench the scorching heat of despair; for who is such as thou, O Virgin Theotokos, our hope?

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Give ear unto the voice of thy servant who needs thy help, O Mother of God, my hope: hear me, and snatch me from temptations.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

The torment of the multitude of transgressions has come upon us, bearing ruinous death. But save thy servants, O Theotokos: for thou art able.

[f. 84rA] *Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of righteousness, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*

Overcoming all those who war against me and fiercely strive to seize my soul, preserve me, O Sovereign Lady, have mercy, and save me: for unto thee do I flee, thy servant.

*Troparion:*

Delivering me from a deceitful tongue, O my Good Protectress, show me unharmed also by worldly deeds: for thou canst do much, as thou art the Mother of the Creator.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Knowing thee as a painless Healer, I, feeble one, call in spirit and with lips: heal me, O Sovereign Lady, have mercy, and save me: for unto thee do I flee, thy servant.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Leave me not to be betrayed unto temptations, O Mother of our God: but from every sorrow and human malice preserve me unharmed. For thou art a Helper unto us all.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Thou, my Lord, hast come into the world as Light, the Holy Light Who turnest back from the gloom of ignorance those who praise Thee in faith.

*Troparion:*

Direct, O Pure One, the prayer of thy servant unto the Lord thy Son: that I may find release from my many transgressions.

[f. 84vA] *Troparion:*

Deliver me from passions and calamities, O Bride of God: for God has truly appointed thee as purification for my lowliness.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Thou art my protection and continual praise, O Sovereign Lady Theotokos: for thou dost in no wise despise those who flee unto thee.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Have mercy, O Pure One, upon those that honor thy Childbirth, and deliver [us] from torment and human bitterness: for thou hast the power so to do.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from demonic blood by the Blood that mercifully flowed from Thy side.

*Troparion:*

Thou thyself art strength unto me, O Most Pure Sovereign Lady, in the unexpected sorrows that sorely encompass me, and I cry unto thee: for thou art a great Protectress unto thy servant.

*Troparion:*

Heal the wounds of my soul, O Most Pure One; defend me, O Virgin Maiden, and deliver thy servant from slander, from assault, and from the perversity of the enemy.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Crush the unjust assailants who attack me, who ever flee unto thee, and leave me not to perish: for all things are possible unto thee, O Pure One, as the Maiden of God.

*Both now...*

[f. 85rA] *Theotokion:*

Overcome the raging wave of my soul: for a multitude of transgressions, temptations, and sorrows, O Sovereign Lady, have risen up against me; but do thou thyself save me.

*Then, Lord, have mercy (3 times). Glory... Both now...*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*[^49]

Having darkened my prodigal mind with many transgressions, I cry unto thy mighty protection, O Theotokos: enlighten the pupils of my soul, make the radiant dawn of repentance shine upon me, and clothe me in the armor of light, O Pure Mother of God.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

In the furnace the fire slew the Chaldeans, but the God of righteousness gave rest unto the righteous Abrahamic children; exceedingly praised Lord and God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*

Swiftly destroying the violence of the Hagarenes with the sword of thy prayers, O Mary, preserve thy people and thy flock, who cry unto thy Son: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou![^50]

*Troparion:*

O Tabernacle of equal majesty, accept me who flee unto thee, lest the enemy who desires to destroy me seize me, as I cry: Exalted God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

O Mother of God, Mary, swiftly anticipate thy servant, who is submerged in the tempestuous waves of temptations [f. 85vA] and has no helper, yet cries unto thee: O Hope of the ends of the earth, have mercy upon me!

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Dispel now human reasonings, O Good Theotokos, since they are the cause of sins, through thy divine prayers; and deliver thy servants from grievous temptation and from every harm.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

O Redeemer of all, Almighty One, having descended into the midst of the flame, Thou didst bedew those who acted piously, and didst teach them to sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*

A lawless nation has come upon us, boasting to destroy thy servants; having consumed them, O Most Pure One, shelter those that cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*

Thy many compassions mercifully save us, O only Mother of God, from sinful judgment and diverse temptations: for having given birth unto God, thou hast mercy upon His world.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Since thou art my strength and help, I fear not the rage of enemies: but I sing of thee, O Sovereign Lady, and cry unto thy Son: Bless the Lord, all ye works!

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Have tenderhearted compassion now upon my supplication, and grant me joy instead of sorrow: that I may sing of thee, O Sovereign Lady, and cry unto thy Son: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

[f. 86rA] *Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Eve through the disease of disobedience brought in the curse, but thou, O Virgin Theotokos, through the blossoming of thy bearing in the womb didst make blessing bud forth unto the world: therefore we all magnify thee.

*Troparion:*

Having sharpened a weapon against us, the deceitful, lawless Arabian takes counsel; but do thou, O Virgin Theotokos, by the power of the Cross and of thy prayers, arm thy servants against him: therefore we proclaim thy glory.[^51]

*Troparion:*

Strength against enemies was given unto thee, O Sovereign Lady, and deliverance from calamities unto me. What then shall I offer unto thee, I am at a loss; nevertheless, the thanksgiving that I have I offer unto thee: accept this now, and save me.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

All-radiant Mother of the Creator of all, Consolation of the sorrowful, Protectress of the drowning, Defender of the overwhelmed: do thou preserve me unto the end of my life.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Unto me who am oppressed by many sins and calamities, despise me not now, O All-Lauded One; I offer unto thee a sacrifice of praise, crying diligently unto thee: Holy Theotokos, help me, for glorifying thee I conclude my song.

*Then, It is truly meet: Trisagion, and after the Our Father: and the rest as usual: and the Dismissal.*

[f. 86vA] *(Woodcut: Decorative headpiece with emblems and the Holy Cross)*

## ON SUNDAY AT SMALL COMPLINE (TONE 4)

*On Sunday at Compline,*[^52]

*Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*Ode 1, Tone 4. Heirmos:*

Israel of old traversed the abyss of the Red Sea with unwetted feet; and by Moses' arms outstretched in the likeness of the Cross, he conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Accept from the soul my supplication, O Most Pure Sovereign Lady, who didst bear God in the flesh: for unto thee have I fled as a mighty Helper, that I may not be disappointed of my hope.

*Troparion:*

I fall down before thee as a servant, O Most Pure Mother of God: as thou hast great boldness, deliver me from all sorrows by thy prayers, interceding with thy Son.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Submerged by the waves of the sea of life, and held fast by fierce temptations, I have hastened unto the storm-free haven of thy protection; therefore deliver me, O Theotokos, from fierce evils.

*Both now...*

[f. 87rA] *Theotokion:*

Look down upon thy servant with thy tenderhearted and quiet eye, and make haste to hear, O Good One, fulfilling the supplication of thy servant, O Most Pure One, and destroying deceitful counsels.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thy Church makes glad in Thee, O Christ, crying: Thou art my strength, O Lord, my refuge, and my establishment.

*Troparion:*

A Helper against enemies and an Ally in battles, O Sovereign Lady, art thou unto the faithful, and a Refuge unto those in grief.

*Troparion:*

Implore Him Whom thou didst bear in the flesh, the God of all, as thy Son: to grant me release from my evils and from worldly assaults.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Look down mercifully upon our lowliness, O Sovereign Lady: that we thy servants may be delivered from impending wrath.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Being ever saved by thy protection from all sorrows, O Good Sovereign Lady, we offer praise unto thy Son.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

I heard report of Thee, O God, and was afraid; I understood Thy works, and was filled with wonder, O Lord: for the earth was full of Thy praise.

*Troparion:*

As thou hast boldness unto thy Son, O Pure Mother of God, from present [f. 87vA] temptation deliver me, destroying the persistent counsels of the enemies that ever war against me.

*Troparion:*

Ye choirs of the higher Ranks, Martyrs, and Righteous Ones with Apostles, the divine assembly of Prophets, and the Venerable, together with the Theotokos and Mother, implore Christ for our sake.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Accept Thy most glorious Mother, O Christ, praying for the world, crying mercifully unto Thee: O My Son, accept My supplication, and stay the wrath that overhangs the earth.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

I fall down before thee, O Theotokos, and pray from the depth of my heart: turn away the present temptation from me, that having been delivered from fierce evils, I may offer a hymn unto thy brightness.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

The ungodly shall not see Thy glory, O Christ; but we, rising early from the night, sing of Thee, O Only-Begotten, the Radiance of the Father's glory of the Godhead, O Merciful One.

*Troparion:*

O Hope of the earthly and Protection, Pure One, have tenderhearted compassion upon our lowliness, we pray, and free us from impending wrath.

*Troparion:*

Holding thy prayer as an insurmountable rampart, O Pure One, we cry unto thee: Have tenderhearted compassion, O Sovereign Lady, and drive away visible enemies.

*Glory...*

[f. 88rA] *Troparion:*

Who ever praise the hope that we have in thee, O Most Pure One, may we not be put to shame; we pray with tears, and bow down before thy goodness.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

With thy most pure hand, O All-Lauded One, scatter the enemies that rise up against us: that the wretched ones may understand that we have placed our hope in thee.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Prefiguring the three-day burial, the Prophet Jonah cried, praying in the sea-monster: From corruption save me, O Jesus, King of powers!

*Troparion:*

Cleanse our sins, O Lover of mankind, we pray, through the prayers of her that seedlessly bore Thee: for for our sake, O Word, Thou didst pour out Thy Precious Blood.

*Troparion:*

An evil assembly has gathered against us of those who unrighteously war against us, O Bride of God; but cast them down, as thou didst of old through Samson.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Hear our supplication, O Sovereign Lady, we pray, and calm the storm-tossed waves of diverse sicknesses, whereby enemies have gathered against us.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Thou Who didst turn water into wine in Cana of Galilee, O Christ, transform my sorrow into joy, as Thou art generous, and change weeping into gladness, through compassion for the sake of the Theotokos.

[f. 88vA] *Then, Lord, have mercy (3 times). Glory... Both now...*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*[^53]

Having darkened my prodigal mind with many transgressions, I cry unto thy mighty protection, O Theotokos: enlighten the pupils of my soul, make the radiant dawn of repentance shine upon me, and clothe me in the armor of light, O Pure Mother of God.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

Deliver us not up utterly for Thy Name's sake, neither break Thou Thy covenant, and take not Thy mercy away from us, O Lord God of our fathers, praised above all unto the ages.

*Troparion:*

Being in diverse temptations and sorrows, unto thee, O Pure One, my salvation, have I now fled, crying: May I not turn back put to shame from my expectation, but hear and deliver me from the nets of those that hunt me.

*Troparion:*

As a servant I, wretched one, bow my neck, and cry from my heart; stretching forth my hands, I bend the knees, and implore thee, Most Pure Virgin: to be delivered from the sorrows that ever come upon me from the assault of the stranger.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

O Most Glorious and Pure Mary, praise of the earthly, grant thy help unto us who pray and piously bow down before thy Childbirth: for we have acquired no other hope or protectress besides thee.

*Both now...*

[f. 89rA] *Theotokion:*

Having borne God beyond understanding and beyond all nature, O Mary unwedded, Protectress of the faithful: deliver those that honor thee pure and unharmed from sudden temptations, and from human enemies, both visible and invisible.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Stretching forth his hands, Daniel shut the yawning mouths of the lions in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*

I dare not lift up my hands unto thy Son, O Pure One: for I am wholly defiled. Therefore do I venture to flee unto thee, O Sovereign Lady: intercede with the merciful and forgiving God, that we may be delivered from adversary enemies who afflict us.

*Troparion:*

Mind and heart and soul have I placed upon thee, O Most Pure One. Therefore have tenderhearted compassion, O Pure Sovereign Lady, falling down before the Merciful One on behalf of me, lowly and impatient: that He may save me from every distress, and plague, and sorrow.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

With Thine armor, as Thou art mighty, overthrow those that war against us, O Lord, and grant victory, O Master, unto those that hope in Thee in faith: the Theotokos implores Thee, together with the Forerunner John, and the Choir of Apostles, and Thy Martyrs.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

The joy of the Annunciation did Gabriel once bring unto thee, O Pure Virgin, and the mourning of our First Mother [f. 89vA] was dissolved by thy Childbirth. Therefore, having cleansed the despondency of my soul, do thou thyself show me unashamed through thy prayers.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

A cornerstone was cut out without hands from an unquarried mountain, even from thee, O Virgin: Christ, Who joined together the sundered natures. Therefore, rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Theotokos.

*Troparion:*

Show forth thy help in affliction, O Virgin Theotokos; diligently incline thine ear, and hear those that cry fervently, and change fierce evils, delivering us by thy prayers.

*Troparion:*

I am wholly held fast by sloth, and am in the depth of despair because of my transgressions. Therefore stretch forth a hand unto me, O Virgin Mother, even as Christ unto Peter, and deliver me from the depth of transgressions.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Subdue, O Virgin, an unbridled and heartless tongue, sharpened like an arrow, that seeks to shoot and slay me, and melt it like wax, and make its counsel vain.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Dispel all the counsels of those armed against us, O Mother of God the Most High, and fill with joy those that hope in thee: that we all may fervently proclaim thy protection.

*Then, It is truly meet: Trisagion, and the rest as usual: and the Dismissal.*

[f. 90rA] *(Woodcut: Decorative headpiece showing the face of a winged Seraph between floral rosettes)*

## ON MONDAY AT SMALL COMPLINE (TONE 4)

*On Monday at Compline,*[^54]

*Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*Ode 1, Tone 4. Heirmos:*

Israel of old traversed the abyss of the Red Sea with unwetted feet; and by Moses' arms outstretched in the likeness of the Cross, he conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Verse: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion:*

Thou who alone protectest in temptations and sorrows, as thou art All-Good, accept the fervent supplications from our hearts of those that warmly flee unto thy protection, O Most Pure One.

*Troparion:*

Having found thee as a storm-free haven, I, wretched one, driving away tempestuous and distressing assaults, sing unto thee a hymn of thanksgiving, O Mother unwedded.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Beholding me with thy merciful and gentle eye, O Mother of God, held fast in tribulation and sorrow, swiftly set me free: for I call upon thee for help.

*Both now...*

[f. 90vA] *Theotokion:*

Both Eve and Adam were delivered from condemnation through thee, O Pure One; with them, turn now the sorrowful tears of us who fall down before thee into joy, and free us from misfortunes.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

The bow of the mighty has grown weak, and the feeble have girded themselves with power; therefore my heart is glad in the Lord.

*Troparion:*

Having acquired thee as a strong weapon and a rampart, I conquer the hosts of adversaries, and sing of thy greatness, O Theotokos Unwedded.

*Troparion:*

Thou dost destroy the furnace of griefs and quench the scorching heat of despair; for who is such as thou, O Virgin Theotokos, our hope?

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Give ear unto the cry of thy servant who needs thy help, O Mother of God, my hope: hear me, and make haste to save me.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Look down, O Pure One, to save me: for by divine behest thou hast incarnated Him Who upholds all things, God the Word, beyond word and understanding.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of righteousness, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

[f. 91rA] *Troparion:*

I conquer all who war against me, who fiercely strive to seize my soul: preserve me unharmed, O Sovereign Lady, that rejoicing I may glorify thee.

*Troparion:*

Delivering me from a deceitful tongue, O Good Protectress, show me unharmed by worldly deeds: for thou canst do much, being the Mother of the Creator.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Knowing thee as a painless Healer of the infirm, I call in spirit and with lips: heal me, O Sovereign Lady, have mercy on me, and save me: for unto thee do I flee, thy servant.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

As the praise of angels and men we all sing of thee, O Virgin Mary, and faithfully pray: pray, O Sovereign Lady, that we may be delivered from every sorrow.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Thou, my Lord, hast come into the world as Light, the Holy Light Who turnest back from the gloom of ignorance those who praise Thee in faith.

*Troparion:*

Direct, O Pure One, the prayer of thy servant unto the Lord thy Son: that I may find release from many transgressions.

*Troparion:*

Deliver me from passions and calamities, O Bride of God: for God has appointed thee as Intercessor with Himself for my lowliness.

*Glory...*

[f. 91vA] *Troparion:*

Thou art my Protectress and continual praise, O Sovereign Lady Theotokos: for thou dost in no wise despise one held fast in griefs.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*

Ask now remission of our transgressions, and deliver from temptations and unclean passions by thy prayers, O All-Lauded One.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from demonic blood by the Blood that mercifully flowed from Thy side.

*Troparion:*

Thou thyself art my strength, O Most Pure Sovereign Lady, in the unexpected sorrows that have sorely found me; therefore I cry unto thee: for thou art a great Helper unto thy servant.

*Troparion:*

Heal the wounds of my soul, O Sovereign Lady; defend me, O Virgin Maiden, and deliver thy servant from slander, assault, and unrighteous perversity.

*Glory...*

*Troparion:*

Crush the unrighteous assailants who attack me, who ever flee unto thee, O Pure One, and leave me not to perish: for all things are possible unto thee, O Maiden of God.

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:*
