O Lord, Heavenly King, Comforter, Spirit of truth: have compassion and have mercy upon me, Thy sinful servant, and loose unto me, unworthy one, and forgive all whereinsoever I have sinned against Thee this day as a man, or rather not as a man, but even worse than a beast; my voluntary sins and involuntary, known and unknown, which are from youth and from evil habit, which come of presumption and faintheartedness. If I have sworn by Thy Name, or blasphemed It in my thought; or reproached [f. 229rA] anyone in my wrath; or grieved anyone, or became angered at anything; or lied, or slept unseasonably; or if a poor man came unto me, and I despised him; or if I grieved my brother, or quarreled; or judged anyone; or became haughty, or grew proud; or when standing at prayer, my mind was moved by the malice of this world; or thought perversity; or overate, or drank to excess, or laughed mindlessly; or thought evil; or saw another's beauty and was wounded thereby in heart; or spoke unfitting things; or mocked my brother's sin: while mine own transgressions are countless; or neglected prayer; or did some other evil thing, remember it not; for all these things and greater than these have I done. Have mercy upon me, O my Creator, [f. 229vA] O Master, upon me, Thy fainthearted and unworthy servant; and remit unto me, and loose, and forgive me, as Thou art good and the Lover of mankind; that in peace I may lie down, fall asleep, and take rest, prodigal, sinful, and wretched though I am; and that I may worship, hymn, and glorify Thine all-honorable Name, with the Father and His Only-begotten Son, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Prayer 4:*[^419]

O Lord our God, whereinsoever I have sinned this day in word, deed, and thought, as Thou art good and the Lover of mankind, forgive me. Grant me peaceful and untroubled sleep. Send Thy Guardian Angel to overshadow and preserve me from every evil: for Thou art the Guardian of our souls and our bodies, and unto Thee do we send up glory:

[f. 230rA] to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Prayer 5:*[^420]

O Lord our God, in Whom we believe, and Whose Name we call upon above every name: grant unto us as we go to sleep relaxation of soul and body; preserve us from every phantom and dark pleasure; stop the assault of passions; quench the burning rebellion of the flesh; grant us to live chastely in deeds and words, that embracing a virtuous conversation, we may not fall away from Thy promised blessings: for blessed art Thou unto the ages. Amen.

*Prayer 6, of Peter the Monk of the Monastery of Studios, unto the Most Holy Theotokos:*[^421]

Unto thee, Most Pure Mother of God, do I wretched fall down and pray: Behold, O Queen, that unceasingly [f. 230vA] I sin, and anger Thy Son and my God; and though I often repent, I am found a liar before God; and repenting, I tremble lest the Lord strike me, yet after a little while I do the same again. Knowing this, my Sovereign Lady, Queen and Theotokos, I pray thee to have mercy, to strengthen me, and to grant that I may do good works; for I know, my Sovereign Lady Theotokos, that I utterly hate mine evil deeds, and with all my mind I love the law of my God: but I know not, O All-pure Queen, how it is that what I hate, that I also love, and good things I transgress.[^422] Suffer not, O All-pure One, my will to be accomplished, for it is not pleasing unto God; but let the will of Thy Son and my God be done, that He may save me, and give me understanding, and grant the grace of the Holy Spirit, that from henceforth I may cease from foul deeds, and for the rest live in the commandments of Thy Son:

[f. 231rA] unto Whom is due all glory, honor, and dominion, with His unoriginate Father, and His All-Holy, Good, and Life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Prayer 7:*[^423]

O Good Mother of the Good King, Most Pure and Blessed Theotokos Mary: pour out the mercy of thy Son and our God upon my passionate soul, and by thy prayers guide me unto good deeds, that I may pass the remaining time of my life without blemish, and through thee may find Paradise, O Virgin Theotokos, who alone art pure and blessed.

*Prayer 8, unto the Holy Guardian Angel:*[^424]

O Angel of Christ, my holy Guardian and protector of my soul and body: forgive me all whereinsoever I have sinned this day, and deliver me from every malice of the opposing [f. 231vA] enemy, that I in no sin may provoke my God to anger; but pray for me, a sinful and unworthy servant, that thou mayest show me forth worthy of the goodness and mercy of the All-Holy Trinity, and of the Mother of my Lord Jesus Christ, and of all the Saints. Amen.

*Kontakion of the Theotokos:*[^425]

To thee, our Champion General, we thy servants ascribe the victory: having been delivered from evils, we offer hymns of thanksgiving unto thee, O Theotokos; but as thou hast invincible might, deliver us from all dangers, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Bride unwedded!

*O most glorious, Ever-Virgin Theotokos Mary, Mother of Christ our God: receive our prayers, and present them unto thy Son and our God, that for thy sake He may save and enlighten our souls.*

*All my hope do I place in thee, O Mother of God; [f. 232rA] keep me under thy protection.*

*O Virgin Theotokos, despise me not a sinner, in need of thy help and thy protection: for in thee hath my soul hoped, and have mercy upon me.*

*The Father is my hope, the Son is my refuge, and the Holy Spirit is my protector: O Holy Trinity, glory to Thee!*

*Then, "It is truly meet...": Glory: Both now: Lord, have mercy (2). O Lord, bless: and the Dismissal:*

> **Priest:** "O Lord Jesus Christ our God, through the prayers of Thine all-pure Mother, and of our venerable Fathers Anthony and Theodosius of the Caves,[^426] and of all the Saints, have mercy on us. Amen."

*Prayer of Saint John of Damascus, pointing toward one's bed, say:*[^427]

O Master, Lover of mankind, shall this bed indeed be my tomb? Or wilt Thou yet enlighten my wretched soul with day? Behold, the tomb lies before me; behold, death [f. 232vA] stands before me; I fear Thy judgment, O Lord, and the endless torment, yet I cease not doing evil; Thee, the Lord my God, do I ever provoke to anger, and Thine all-pure Mother, and all the Heavenly Powers, and my holy Guardian Angel. For I know, O Lord, that I am not worthy of Thy love for mankind, but am worthy of all condemnation and torment: but, O Lord, whether I will or will not, save me. For if Thou savest the righteous, it is no great thing, and if Thou hast mercy upon the pure, it is nothing marvelous, for they are worthy of Thy mercy. But upon me a sinner make Thy mercy marvelous; herein show forth Thy love for mankind, lest mine evil overcome Thine ineffable goodness and compassion; and even as Thou wilt, order all things concerning me.

*A prostration unto the face of the earth, after the whole Rule.*[^428]

[f. 233rA] *(Woodcut: The Holy Trinity enthroned in glory among clouds, God the Father depicted with papal-style tiara holding a sceptre, God the Son holding the Cross, and the Holy Spirit as a Dove radiating light above, surrounded by Seraphim and Cherubim)*[^429]

I worship Thee, O Most Holy Trinity, consubstantial, life-creating, and undivided, Father, Son, and Holy Spirit; I believe in Thee and confess, I glorify, give thanks, praise, honor, and extol Thee, and I pray: have mercy upon me, Thine unprofitable creature, for Thy Name's sake.

*Say this thrice, with a prostration. Thereafter:*

Most Holy Lady, Virgin Theotokos, Mother of my Lord and God, who art the only protectress, help, and hope of my salvation after God: blessed art thou among women, and blessed [f. 233vA] is the fruit of thy womb, with Whom I call thee blessed, honor, magnify, and pray thee: remember me, my Mediatress, in thy maternal prayers unto God.

*Prostration 1:*

O All-honorable and Life-creating Cross of the Lord, by the power of Christ crucified upon thee, strengthen me against all visible and invisible enemies, preserving me from temptations of the flesh and of the soul.

*Prostration 1:*

All ye holy Heavenly Ranks: Angels, Archangels, Principalities, Authorities, Powers, Dominions, Thrones, Cherubim, and Seraphim: enlighten, sanctify, and preserve me by your intercession and protection.

*Prostration 1:*

O holy Angel of Christ, Guardian of my soul [f. 234rA] and of my body, cover me with the wings of thy goodness from every evil operation of the demons, guiding me into the path of salvation, and pray to God for me a sinner.

*Prostration 1:*

Holy, glorious Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist of the Lord, John, and all ye holy Prophets, Forefathers, and Fathers: pray to God for me a sinner, that I may be saved by your prayers.

*Prostration 1:*

Holy, glorious, and all-praised Apostles of Christ, the Twelve and the Seventy: pray to Christ the Lord, Who gave unto you the Holy Spirit to remit sins, that by the same Holy Spirit He may remit mine offences, kept bound through the years.[^430]

*Prostration 1:*

Holy hierarchs of Christ: Basil the Great, Gregory [f. 234vA] the Theologian, and John Chrysostom, Athanasius and Cyril, Nicholas, Spyridon, Peter, Alexis, and Jonah, Russian wonderworkers,[^431] and the other Hierarchs, Pastors, Teachers, Priests, and Confessors of Christ: pray to God for me a sinner.

*Prostration 1:*

Holy Great-Martyrs of Christ: Stephen, George, Demetrius, Theodore Stratelates, and Theodore the Recruit, Procopius, Artemius, Roman and David, Russian wonderworkers,[^432] and holy female martyrs Thecla, Barbara, Catherine, and Irene, and the other martyrs and confessors of Christ: pray to God for me a sinner.

*Prostration 1:*

Our venerable and God-bearing Fathers Anthony [f. 235rA] and Theodosius, Euthymius, Sabbas, Macarius, Paul, Onuphrius, and Joannicius, Anthony and Theodosius of the Caves,[^433] and our venerable Mothers: Mary, Theodora, Euphrosyne, and the other holy Fathers and Mothers: pray to God for me a sinner.

*Prostration 1:*

Holy Unmercenaries and Wonderworking Physicians: Cosmas and Damian, Cyrus and John, Panteleimon, Hermolaus, Agapitus,[^434] and the other wonderworkers: pray to God for me a sinner.

*Prostration 1:*

All ye holy Saints of God, whose souls even as they are in the hand of God, so also their names are written in the Heavens: pray to God for me a sinner.

[f. 235vA] *Troparia of Compline:*[^435]

Enlighten mine eyes, O Christ God, lest I sleep the sleep of death, lest mine enemy say: I have prevailed against him.[^436]

*Glory:*

Be Thou the protector of my soul, O God, for I walk in the midst of many snares; deliver me from them, and save me, O Good One, as the Lover of mankind.

*Both now:*

The most glorious Mother of Christ, and holier than the holy Angels, let us praise unceasingly with heart and lips, confessing thee to be the Theotokos: for truly hast thou borne for us God incarnate, and dost pray unceasingly for our souls.

*Then kiss thy cross which thou wearest upon thy breast, and cross thy bed therewith from head to foot, likewise also on all sides, saying the Prayer to the Precious Cross:*[^437]

[f. 236rA] *Prayer to the Precious and Life-Creating Cross of the Lord:*[^438]

Let God arise, and let His enemies be scattered; and let them that hate Him flee from before His face. As smoke vanishes, so let them vanish; as wax melts before the fire, so let demons perish from the face of them that love God and mark themselves with the sign of the Cross, and say with gladness: Rejoice, O All-honorable and Life-creating Cross of the Lord, who drivest away demons by the power of our Lord Jesus Christ Who was crucified upon thee, Who descended into Hades and trampled down the power of the devil, and gave thee unto us, His honorable Cross, for the driving away of every enemy and adversary.[^439]

O All-honorable and Life-creating Cross of the Lord: help me with the Holy Lady, Virgin Theotokos, and with all the Saints, unto ages. Amen.

*(Woodcut: Floral ornament vignette)*

---

# ASCETIC HOMILIES AND COLOPHON

[f. 236vA] *(Woodcut: Elaborate typographic headpiece band with floral and geometric arabesques)*

*Colophon of the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra Octoechos-Theotokarion:*[^440]

Unto the Mighty God Who hath done great things for the Virgin Mother of God,[^441] let this canonical blessed hymnody be unto honor and glory, and unto those that read and hear, unto spiritual profit. Amen.

*(Woodcut: Typographic printer's mark and heraldic emblem of the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra printing house, depicting a crowned cherub with outspread wings beneath an imperial baldachin draped with festoons, wreaths, and pendant tassels)*[^442]

[p246RA] *Daily Examination of Conscience before Sleep (Contemporary 18th-century manuscript addition on facing unnumbered recto):*[^443]

Before thou liest down upon thy bed, consider all these things and pass through them in thy memory:

First: Give thanks unto Almighty God, that He hath granted thee by His grace to pass through the past day in life and in health, saying:

I give thanks unto Thee, O Lord God, my most good Giver of life, that in this past day, wherein many have passed from this life unto death, and especially unto eternal death, Thou hast been well-pleased to preserve me, Thine unworthy servant, whole and unharmed until this night. Blessed, therefore, be Thy Name across all days and nights, and unto ages.

Second: Settle accounts with thine own self, and make an examination of thy conscience, going over and recounting in detail all the daytime hours, beginning from the time when thou didst arise from thy bed and bringing to thy remembrance: whither thou didst go, what thou didst do unto anyone, and what thou didst counsel, and all thy deeds, words, and thoughts produced by thee from morning even until evening, keeping all these things in mind...[^444]

# FOOTNOTES AND SCHOLARLY COMMENTARY



[^1]: The front flyleaf contains ink provenance shelfmarks and calligraphic cursive ownership notes of the eighteenth century, including references to Deacon Elijah ("З книгъ Іллїѧ дїакона...") and the archival cataloguing record "1795 года Но: 3."

[^2]: The architectural woodcut border of the master title page depicts Christ Pantocrator flanked by angels at the summit, the Dormition of the Mother of God directly beneath, lateral vertical registers with medallic portraits of Saint Prince Vladimir, Princess Olga, the Holy Passion-Bearers Boris and Gleb, the Venerable Anthony and Theodosius of the Caves, and Saint Andrew the First-Called, and a detailed panoramic engraving of the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra with churches and bastions at the base, signed with the engraver's monogram "A. K." and dated 1707 ("Аѱо҃з.").

[^3]: This full-page woodcut frontispiece is the work of the renowned Kyivan Lavra master woodcut artist Marko (signed "МАРКО" in the lower right foreground), depicting the Dormition with Christ receiving the soul of His Mother amid the assembled Apostles. Beneath the engraving is printed the Troparion of the Dormition in Tone 1 (*Въ Рождествѣ Дѣвство сохранила еси...*).

[^4]: Archimandrite Ioannikii Sennutovych served as Archimandrite of the Holy Dormition Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra from 1715 until his death in 1729, overseeing an active period of liturgical printing and scholarly publication at the Lavra's historic press.

[^5]: The full-page woodcut frontispiece on fol. [Prelim 3v] depicts Christ Pantocrator blessing and holding the open Gospel (inscribed with Matt. 11:28–29) under a classical archway. Below is the Johannine declaration "I am the door: by Me if any man enter in, he shall be saved" (John 10:9) and an Imperial double-headed eagle framed by baroque acanthus scrollwork, symbolizing the official patronage and printing privilege of the Lavra under the Kyivan metropolitanate.

[^6]: A 19th-century cursive Cyrillic library annotation at the foot of fol. 5r records that the volume belonged to the library of the Trinity-St. Sergius Lavra in 1859 ("Принадлежитъ библіотекѣ Троицкой Сергіевой Лавры. 1859 года.").

[^7]: Church Slavonic four-line emblematic verse (*virsh*) set below the full-page woodcut engraving of the Mother of God surrounded by twelve Old Testament prefigurations.

[^8]: Liturgical incipit marking the opening of the weekly cycle of Marian Canons for Tone 1 at Small Compline (*Павечерница* / *Apodeipnon*). In the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra printed tradition, each Tone provides a distinct Marian canon for each day of the week, beginning with Saturday evening (Sunday Compline).

[^9]: Slavonic: *чувственныхъ и мысленныхъ* ("sensible and intelligible"), translated in the canonical register as "visible and invisible" in reference to demonic and bodily foes.

[^10]: Liturgical incipit for the Sunday evening Marian canon (chanted at Small Compline on Sunday, serving the liturgical day of Monday). In Octoechos usage, the weekly cycle shifts to the angels and repentance.

[^11]: Nineteenth-century Russian cursive archival inscription in ink at bottom margin: *Принадлежитъ библіотекѣ Троицкой Сергіевой Лавры. 1859 года.* ("Belongs to the library of the Trinity-St. Sergius Lavra. In the year 1859.").

[^12]: Liturgical incipit for the Monday evening Marian canon (chanted at Small Compline on Monday, serving the liturgical day of Tuesday). In the Octoechos cycle, this service is aligned with repentance and the forerunner.

[^13]: Liturgical incipit for the Tuesday evening Marian canon (chanted at Small Compline on Tuesday, serving the liturgical day of Wednesday). In the Octoechos cycle, this service is aligned with the Precious and Life-Giving Cross.

[^14]: Liturgical incipit for the Wednesday evening Marian canon (chanted at Small Compline on Wednesday, serving the liturgical day of Thursday). In the Octoechos cycle, this service is aligned with the Holy Apostles and Saint Nicholas.

[^15]: Liturgical incipit for the Thursday evening Marian canon (chanted at Small Compline on Thursday, serving the liturgical day of Friday). In the Octoechos cycle, this service is dedicated to the Precious and Life-Giving Cross and the Passion of Christ.

[^16]: Liturgical incipit for the Friday evening Marian canon (chanted at Small Compline on Friday, serving the liturgical day of Saturday). In the Octoechos cycle, this service is aligned with All Saints and the remembrance of the faithful departed.

[^17]: Direct liturgical witness to the Classical Patristic theology of sacred images defined by Saint Basil the Great (*On the Holy Spirit*, 18.45: 'The honor rendered to the icon passes to the prototype') and dogmatized at the Seventh Ecumenical Council (Nicaea II, 787). The Slavonic text (*Печа́ть тѧ̀ Влч҃нꙋ... ꙗ҃кѡ первоѡ́бразнꙋ чтꙋ̀*) explicitly asserts the relative veneration (*proskynesis*) due to the Marian icon.

[^18]: Liturgical incipit for the Saturday evening Marian canon of Tone 2 (chanted at Small Compline on Saturday, serving the liturgical day of Sunday). The rubric explicitly directs this canon to be chanted by those suffering under slander, sorrow, and tribulations (*его́же и҆ ѿ ѡ҆клевета́нїѧ въ печа́лехъ и҆ напа́стехъ сꙋ́щїи да пою́тъ*).

[^19]: Typological allusion to the traitorous counselor Ahithophel (2 Sam. 15:31; 17:1–23), whose cunning conspiracy against King David was brought to naught through prayer ('O Lord, turn the counsel of Ahithophel into foolishness'). In the liturgical context of this canon against slander and unjust persecution, Ahithophel serves as the archetypal conspirator whose evil design is shattered through Marian intercession.

[^20]: Liturgical incipit for the Sunday evening Marian canon of Tone 2 (chanted at Small Compline on Sunday, serving the liturgical day of Monday). In the weekly Octoechos cycle, Monday is dedicated to the Bodiless Powers of Heaven (the Holy Angels and Archangels).

[^21]: Direct Scriptural and liturgical typological witness to the Seraphic tongs and the burning coal of Isaiah 6:6–7. In Patristic Mariology, the tongs (*λαβίς* / *клѣщѣ*) prefigure the Theotokos, who bore the uncontainable fire of the Divine Word without being consumed.

[^22]: Liturgical incipit for the Monday evening Marian canon of Tone 2 (chanted at Small Compline on Monday, serving the liturgical day of Tuesday). In the weekly Octoechos cycle, Tuesday is dedicated to the Holy Forerunner and Baptist John, the archetype of repentance and monastic asceticism.

[^23]: Direct liturgical witness to the Seventh Ecumenical Council (Nicaea II, 787) and Orthodox iconodulic dogma. The Slavonic text (*ра́звѣ то́чїю за́вистїю ѿмета́ющїеся ѿ тебѐ, и҆ твое́мꙋ непокланѧ́ющїся ѡ҆́бразꙋ начерта́нномꙋ*) explicitly anathematizes the iconoclastic rejection of the inscribed Marian image (*ѡ҆́бразъ начерта́нный* / *χαρακτήρ* / *εἰκών*), declaring that perdition belongs solely to those who refuse relative veneration (*proskynesis*) to the sacred icon of the Mother of God.

[^24]: Liturgical incipit for the Tuesday evening Marian canon of Tone 2 (chanted at Small Compline on Tuesday, serving the liturgical day of Wednesday). In the Octoechos cycle, Wednesday is consecrated to the commemoration of the Betrayal of Judas and the Passion of Christ on the Precious Cross.

[^25]: The printed Slavonic text reads 'въ разтрѣхова́нїе' (or 'разстрѣхова́нїе'), an unusual Ruthenian Church Slavonic formation denoting extreme perturbation, terror, or scattering into sinful distractions (from *страхъ* / *стрѣх-* or *грѣхъ*).

[^26]: The heading specifies the Small Compline service for Wednesday in Tone 2 (*Въ сре́дꙋ на павече́рнїи*).

[^27]: The melodic model heading in the printed text gives the abbreviated incipit *Подо́бенъ: Бж҃їю бы́хомъ:*, corresponding to the canonical Tone 2 automelon *Боже́ственныя бы́хомъ о́бщницы* / *Θείας γεγόναμεν* ("We have become partakers of the divine [nature]").

[^28]: The heading specifies the Small Compline service for Thursday in Tone 2 (*Въ четве́ртокъ на па́вече́рнїи*).

[^29]: In the Ruthenian liturgical tradition, Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Friday evening forms the vigil eve of Saturday, which is dedicated particularly to the All-Holy Theotokos and to the memory of all departed faithful.

[^30]: The incipit of this Sessional Hymn (*Сѣдаленъ*) is an automelon/idiomelon without a specified *podoben* model in the print witness, retaining its direct Tone 2 melody.

[^31]: Fol. 54v bears a full-page woodcut engraving of the Kyiv-Pechersk Mother of God (*Печерська Пресвята Богородиця*) enthroned with the Christ Child, flanked by Archangels Michael and Gabriel, with the Lavra's founders, Venerable Anthony and Theodosius of the Caves, kneeling at her feet.

[^32]: Saturday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Saturday evening belongs liturge-historically to the Resurrectional Sunday vigil office of Tone 3, opening the weekly cycle of the Third Tone.

[^33]: The phrase *пресла́вное твоегѡ́ о́браза ви́дѧще подо́бїе* ("beholding the most glorious likeness of thine icon") reflects the standard Byzantine-Ruthenian iconophile theology affirmed at the Seventh Ecumenical Council (Nicaea II), defending the veneration of sacred icons against iconoclastic heresy.

[^34]: The Sessional Hymn (*Сѣдаленъ*) of Tone 3 at Small Compline is an idiomelon sung to its own melody without a preceding *podoben* rubric.

[^35]: Sunday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Sunday evening belongs liturge-historically to Monday, the beginning of the liturgical week, which commemorates the Holy Incorporeal Bodiless Hosts (Angels) and the penitential call of the Octoechos.

[^36]: The Sessional Hymn (*Сѣдаленъ*) follows the melodic model *Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the Divine Faith"* (Боже́ственныя вѣ́ры), a standard prosomoion of the Third Tone.

[^37]: Monday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Monday evening liturgically begins Tuesday, dedicated in the weekly liturgical cycle of the Octoechos to the Holy Forerunner and Baptist John.

[^38]: Melodic model: *Tone 3, Podoben: "The Beauty of Thy Virginity"* (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства), one of the most prominent Marian automela of Tone 3.

[^39]: Tuesday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Tuesday evening forms the liturgical eve of Wednesday, which commemorates throughout the Byzantine Octoechos the Holy Cross and the Betrayal of the Lord.

[^40]: This Sessional Hymn to the Cross (*Крестобогородичен* / Stavrotheotokion) of Tone 3 is sung as an idiomelon to its own melody without a preceding *podoben* rubric.

[^41]: Wednesday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Wednesday evening liturgically forms the eve of Thursday, dedicated throughout the Byzantine Octoechos to the Holy Apostles and Saint Nicholas of Myra in Lycia.

[^42]: The hymnographer evokes the Dominical parable of the barren fig tree (Luke 13:6–9; Matt. 21:19), in which the unyielding tree symbolizes the impenitent soul deserving of being cut down and cast into fire.

[^43]: This Sessional Hymn (*Сѣдаленъ*) of Wednesday Compline is an idiomelon sung to its own Third Tone melody without an automelon rubric.

[^44]: Thursday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Thursday evening liturgically begins Friday, the day dedicated across the Byzantine liturgical cycle to the Passion of the Lord and the Adoration of the Precious and Life-Giving Cross.

[^45]: This Sessional Hymn to the Cross (*Крестобогородичен* / Stavrotheotokion) of Thursday Compline is sung as an idiomelon without a podoben model, meditating upon the Mother of God lamenting beneath the Cross on Friday eve.

[^46]: Friday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Friday evening liturgically inaugurates Saturday, dedicated across the Octoechos to all Saints and to the memory of all departed Orthodox Christians.

[^47]: This Sessional Hymn (*Сѣдаленъ*) of Friday Compline in Tone 3 is an idiomelon sung to its own melody, confessing the unconfused two natures of the incarnate Word and the perpetual virginity (*semper virgo*) of the Theotokos.

[^48]: Saturday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Saturday evening liturgically inaugurates Sunday, the Day of the Holy Resurrection, opening the liturgical week of the Fourth Tone (*Гласъ 4*).

[^49]: This Sessional Hymn (*Сѣдаленъ*) of Saturday Compline in Tone 4 is sung as an idiomelon to its own Fourth Tone melody, imploring the Theotokos to dispel spiritual darkness and clothe the soul in the Paul-inspired "armor of light" (Rom. 13:12).

[^50]: The troparion explicitly evokes the historical Ruthenian and Ukrainian context of Crimean Tatar and Ottoman Turkish ("Hagarene") slave raids and invasions, petitioning the Mother of God to intervene with the "sword of her prayers" to protect the Orthodox flock.

[^51]: "Arabian" (*Аравлянинъ*, Greek *Ἄραψ*) functions in Byzantine and Ruthenian hymnography as an ethnonymic designation for Islamic Ottoman forces, reflecting the historical raids threatening the southern frontiers of Ruthenia and the wider Christian East.

[^52]: Sunday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Sunday evening liturgically inaugurates Monday, dedicated across the weekly cycle to the Bodiless Hosts (the Holy Angels and Archangels).

[^53]: This Sessional Hymn is repeated verbatim from the preceding service (Saturday Compline, Fol. 85r), serving here again as the meditative midpoint kathisma of Sunday Compline in Tone 4.

[^54]: Monday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Monday evening liturgically introduces Tuesday, dedicated across the Octoechos to the Holy Prophet, Forerunner and Baptist of the Lord, John.

[^55]: This Sessional Hymn (*Сѣдаленъ*) of Monday Compline in Tone 4 petitions the Theotokos as the unceasing mediatrix before Christ the Lover of mankind, imploring her to accept the evening supplications of her servants.

[^56]: Tuesday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Tuesday evening liturgically inaugurates Wednesday, dedicated across the Octoechos to the Betrayal of the Lord and the veneration of the Precious and Life-Giving Cross.

[^57]: This Sessional Hymn (*Сѣдаленъ*) of Tuesday Compline in Tone 4 is a Stavrotheotokion (*Крестобогородиченъ*), meditating upon the maternal grief of the Virgin Mary at the Cross and the prophetic fulfillment of Symeon's words concerning the sword piercing her soul (Luke 2:35).

[^58]: Wednesday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Wednesday evening liturgically introduces Thursday, dedicated across the Octoechos to the Holy Apostles and to Saint Nicholas the Wonderworker, Archbishop of Myra in Lycia.

[^59]: This Sessional Hymn (*Сѣдаленъ*) of Wednesday Compline in Tone 4 addresses the Theotokos as the invincible Defender (*Предста́тельница нера́тная*), imploring her swift rescue from dangers before the concluding odes of the canon.

[^60]: Thursday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Thursday evening liturgically inaugurates Friday, dedicated across the Octoechos to the Passion of Christ and the veneration of the Precious and Life-Giving Cross.

[^61]: This Sessional Hymn (*Сѣдаленъ*) of Thursday Compline in Tone 4 is a Stavrotheotokion (*Крестобогородиченъ*), portraying the maternal lamentation of the Mother of God standing before the Cross of her Son.

[^62]: Friday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Friday evening liturgically introduces Saturday, dedicated across the Octoechos to the remembrance of All Saints and the commemoration of the faithful departed.

[^63]: This Sessional Hymn (*Сѣдаленъ*) of Friday Compline in Tone 4 extols the Mother of God as our warm protection in afflictions and our reconciliation unto God (*къ Богу премѣненіє*).

[^64]: Saturday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Saturday evening liturgically inaugurates the Sunday of Tone 5, opening the weekly cycle of the Octoechos with the celebration of the Lord's Resurrection and the veneration of the Most Holy Theotokos.

[^65]: This Sessional Hymn (*Сѣдаленъ*) of Saturday Compline in Tone 5 extols the Most Holy Theotokos as the fervent protection and merciful Mother of God the Most High.

[^66]: Sunday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Sunday evening liturgically introduces Monday, dedicated across the Octoechos to the Bodiless Hosts (Angels) and repentance.

[^67]: This Sessional Hymn (*Сѣдаленъ*) of Sunday Compline in Tone 5 expresses penitential mourning before the impending Judgment, entreating the Virgin's intercession for forgiveness.

[^68]: Monday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Monday evening liturgically introduces Tuesday, dedicated across the Octoechos to the Holy Forerunner and Baptist John and to repentance.

[^69]: This Sessional Hymn (*Сѣдаленъ*) of Monday Compline in Tone 5 implores the Mother of God to heal all soul-passions and bodily sicknesses and to quieten straying thoughts for pure prayer.

[^70]: Tuesday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Tuesday evening liturgically introduces Wednesday, dedicated across the Octoechos to the commemoration of the Betrayal of Christ and the veneration of the Precious Cross.

[^71]: An allusion to Isaiah 6:6–7, where the live coal (*оугль*) taken from the altar purges the prophet's lips, patristically typifying Christ in the womb of the Virgin.

[^72]: This Sessional Hymn (*Сѣдаленъ*) of Tuesday Compline in Tone 5 extols the Virgin through whose Son's Cross the power of idols and demons was overthrown.

[^73]: An allusion to the Song of Songs (Song 1:9, 15; 2:2, etc.), where the Bride is addressed as "my near one" or "my love" (*ближняя моя*), interpreted mariologically of the Theotokos.

[^74]: Wednesday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Wednesday evening liturgically introduces Thursday, dedicated across the Octoechos to the Holy Apostles and Saint Nicholas the Wonderworker.

[^75]: An allusion to the Parable of the Barren Fig Tree (Luke 13:6–9) and Christ's cursing of the barren fig tree (Matthew 21:18–19; Mark 11:12–14), a recurring patristic type of unrepentant sloth.

[^76]: This Sessional Hymn (*Сѣдаленъ*) of Wednesday Compline in Tone 5 extols the Theotokos as the impregnable bulwark, the defeat of hostile barbarians, and the defense of the Orthodox faithful against visible and demonic adversaries.

[^77]: Thursday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Thursday evening liturgically introduces Friday, dedicated across the Octoechos to the commemoration of the Crucifixion and Passion of our Lord Jesus Christ.

[^78]: A patristic-typological title (*Трїста́та*) applied to the Mother of God as the mystical Tabernacle containing the Trinity's uncontainable Word, echoing the Exodus imagery of Exodus 14:7 and 15:4.

[^79]: The Stavrotheotokion (*Крестобогородиченъ*) Sessional Hymn of Thursday Compline in Tone 5 laments the voluntary Crucifixion of Christ between the two thieves as beheld by His grieving Mother.

[^80]: A precise dogmatic formula reflecting the Christological definitions of the Sixth Ecumenical Council (Constantinople III, 680–681), affirming two natural wills and two natural operations (*энергии*) in Christ, undivided and unconfused.

[^81]: Friday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Friday evening liturgically introduces Saturday, dedicated across the Octoechos to All Saints, the Holy Martyrs, and the commemoration of all the faithful departed.

[^82]: An allusion to the Magnificat (Luke 1:48): "For behold, from henceforth all generations shall call me blessed."

[^83]: An allusion to Psalm 45:5 (LXX) / Psalm 46:4: "The streams of the river make glad the city of God, the holy place of the tabernacle of the Most High," applied mariologically to the Theotokos.

[^84]: An allusion to Isaiah 45:8: "Drop down dew, ye heavens, from above, and let the clouds shower righteousness," typifying the Virgin through whom the Sun of Righteousness descended.

[^85]: An allusion to Exodus 15:23–25, where the bitter waters of Marah were sweetened by the tree cast in by Moses, patristically typifying the Cross and the sweetening of bitter human passion through Christ.

[^86]: An allusion to Psalm 13:7 (LXX), Psalm 52:6, and Isaiah 59:20 (cf. Romans 11:26): "There shall come out of Zion the Deliverer."

[^87]: This Sessional Hymn (*Сѣдаленъ*) of Friday Compline in Tone 5 urges the soul steeped in sin and despondency to turn in repentance to the Mother of God before the end.

[^88]: An allusion to Isaiah 19:1: "Behold, the Lord rides upon a swift cloud," interpreted by the Church Fathers of the Virgin Mary bearing Christ.

[^89]: An allusion to Ezekiel 44:1–3, the prophecy of the closed eastern gate through which only the Lord God of Israel enters and goes out, a premier Old Testament type of Mary's perpetual virginity.

[^90]: An allusion to John 1:29: "Behold the Lamb of God, which takes away the sin of the world."

[^91]: An allusion to Isaiah 7:14 and Matthew 1:23 (Emmanuel, "God with us"), combined with Zechariah 6:12 and Luke 1:78, where the Messiah is called "the Orient" or "Dayspring from on high" (*Востокъ* / Ἀνατολή).

[^92]: Saturday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Saturday evening liturgically introduces Sunday, the Lord's Day of the Resurrection, opening Tone 6 of the Octoechos cycle.

[^93]: An allusion to 1 Kings (1 Samuel) 2:1–2, the Song of Hannah: "There is none holy as the Lord... neither is there any rock like our God; my horn is exalted in the Lord."

[^94]: An allusion to Habakkuk 3:2: "O Lord, I have heard Thy report, and was afraid."

[^95]: An allusion to 1 Peter 5:8 and Psalm 21:14 (LXX) / Psalm 22:13: "They gaped upon me with their mouths, as a ravening and a roaring lion."

[^96]: An allusion to Psalm 51:4 (LXX) / Psalm 52:2: "Thy tongue devises mischiefs; like a sharp razor, working deceitfully."

[^97]: The renowned automelon / prosomoion Sessional Hymn of Tone 6 (*Заступнице христіанъ непостыдная* / Προστασία τῶν Χριστιανῶν ἀκαταίσχυντε), traditionally sung also as the Kontakion of the Theotokos throughout the Byzantine rite.

[^98]: An allusion to Daniel 3:49–50 and the Song of the Three Holy Youths in the furnace.

[^99]: An allusion to Psalm 21:13 (LXX) / Psalm 22:12: "Many calves have compassed me; fat bulls have beset me round."

[^100]: An allusion to 3 Kings (1 Kings) 18:38, where fire from the Lord fell and consumed the burnt sacrifice of the Prophet Elijah together with the water poured into the trench.

[^101]: An allusion to Psalm 32:10 (LXX) / Psalm 33:10: "The Lord brings the counsel of the heathen to nought; He makes the devices of the people of none effect."

[^102]: Sunday Small Compline (*Павечеріє мале*) on Sunday evening liturgically introduces Monday, dedicated across the Octoechos to the Bodiless Powers and Holy Angels.

[^103]: An allusion to Hebrews 1:3: "Who being the brightness of His glory, and the express image of His person" (ἀπαύγασμα τῆς δόξης).

[^104]: An allusion to Luke 1:28, the angelic salutation of the Archangel Gabriel to the Virgin Mary at the Annunciation.

[^105]: A precise dogmatic formula on the hypostatic union and two natures of Christ (divine and human) defined at the Council of Chalcedon (451 AD).

[^106]: An allusion to Christ as the "Sun of Righteousness" (Malachi 4:2) and "Light unapproachable" (1 Timothy 6:16).

[^107]: An allusion to Habakkuk 3:2, 18–19 and 1 Corinthians 1:24 ("Christ the power of God").

[^108]: An allusion to Malachi 4:2: "Unto you that fear My name shall the Sun of righteousness arise with healing in His wings."

[^109]: An allusion to Isaiah 26:9: "With my soul have I desired Thee in the night; yea, with my spirit within me will I seek Thee early."

[^110]: An allusion to the Parable of the Good Samaritan (Luke 10:30–35), with the oil and wine applied patristically to the healing ministrations of Christ and the Mother of God.

[^111]: An allusion to Luke 1:35: "The Holy Ghost shall come upon thee, and the power of the Highest shall overshadow thee."

[^112]: An allusion to Jonah 2:6 (LXX): "Yet hast Thou brought up my life from corruption, O Lord my God."

[^113]: An allusion to 1 Corinthians 15:22, 45, 47, Christ as the New Adam restoring fallen humanity.

[^114]: The term *ѡ҆чисти́лище* (Greek ἱλαστήριον) denotes a place or means of expiation/cleansing, echoing Exodus 25:17 and Romans 3:25.

[^115]: An allusion to the devil as a ravening beast barking and seeking whom he may devour (cf. 1 Peter 5:8; Psalm 21:17 LXX / Psalm 22:16).

[^116]: An allusion to Exodus 33:20, John 1:18, and 1 Timothy 6:16, where God is invisible to created beings, yet made visible through the Incarnation of the Word from the Theotokos.

[^117]: An allusion to the curse upon Eve in Genesis 3:16, overturned by the joyful Childbirth of the Virgin Mary.

[^118]: A patristic Christological antithesis between the timeless (*безлѣтный*) eternal generation of the Son from the Father and His temporal (*въ лѣто*) birth in the flesh from the Virgin.

[^119]: Scriptural reference to the passage of Israel through the Red Sea and the destruction of Pharaoh's host in Exodus 14:21–31.

[^120]: Heirmos of Ode 3, Tone 6, drawing upon the Song of Hannah in 1 Kings (1 Samuel) 2:2: "There is none holy as the Lord."

[^121]: Heirmos of Ode 5, Tone 6, drawing upon the early-morning prayer of Isaiah in Isaiah 26:9: "From night until morning my spirit wakes early unto Thee, O God."

[^122]: Heirmos of Ode 6, Tone 6, based on the prayer of the Prophet Jonah from the sea-monster in Jonah 2:2–7.

[^123]: Liturgical invocation of the Virgin Mary as the heavenly Intercessor (*hodataitsa* / μεσίτρια) and Protection (*pokrov* / σκέπη).

[^124]: Heirmos of Ode 7, Tone 6, referencing the Angel who descended into the fiery furnace in Daniel 3:49–50 (LXX / Prayer of the Three Youths).

[^125]: Heirmos of Ode 8, Tone 6, referencing Elijah's sacrifice upon Mount Carmel consumed by divine fire despite being drenched with water (3 Kings [1 Kings] 18:33–38). Note doxological formula: "unto all ages" (Greek: εἰς πάντας τοὺς αἰῶνας).

[^126]: Scriptural allusion to the separation of the sheep and the goats at the Last Judgment in Matthew 25:31–46.

[^127]: Heirmos of Ode 9, Tone 6, drawing upon John 1:18 ("No man hath seen God at any time") and 1 Timothy 6:16.

[^128]: Heirmos of Ode 1, Tone 6, repeating the Exodus 14:21–31 heirmos model.

[^129]: Heirmos of Ode 3, Tone 6, repeating the 1 Kings (1 Samuel) 2:2 heirmos model.

[^130]: Scriptural allusion to Esau selling his birthright for a bowl of pottage in Genesis 25:29–34, cited as an image of spiritual heedlessness.

[^131]: Allusion to the unquenchable fire and the sleepless worm (*vermis non moritur*) of Gehenna in Mark 9:43–48 and Isaiah 66:24.

[^132]: Stavrotheotokion theme embedded within the Compline Sessional Hymn, invoking the sorrow of the Theotokos standing beneath the Cross (John 19:25–27).

[^133]: Scriptural allusion to Nebuchadnezzar commanding the fiery furnace to be heated seven times more than usual in Daniel 3:19.

[^134]: Repetition of the Heirmos of Ode 8, Tone 6 (3 Kings [1 Kings] 18:38).

[^135]: Repetition of the Heirmos of Ode 9, Tone 6 (John 1:18).

[^136]: Traditional hymnographic locus echoing the Sixth Hour Theotokion: "For a mother's prayer hath great power to win the favor of the Master."

[^137]: Scriptural allusion to the sheep placed at the right hand of Christ the King at the Last Judgment in Matthew 25:33–34.

[^138]: Heirmos of Ode 1, Tone 6, drawing upon the Song of Moses in Exodus 15:1–2: "The Lord is my strength and song, and He is become my salvation."

[^139]: Heirmos of Ode 3, Tone 6, drawing upon the Song of Hannah in 1 Kings (1 Samuel) 2:2.

[^140]: Heirmos of Ode 4, Tone 6, drawing upon the prayer of the Prophet Habakkuk in Habakkuk 3:2.

[^141]: Scriptural allusion to Psalm 50:9 (LXX / Psalm 51:7): "Thou shalt wash me, and I shall be made whiter than snow."

[^142]: Heirmos of Ode 5, Tone 6, drawing upon Isaiah 26:9.

[^143]: Heirmos of Ode 6, Tone 6, drawing upon the prayer of Jonah in Jonah 2:2–6.

[^144]: Scriptural allusion to Matthew 25:33, 41 regarding the goats placed on the left hand at the Last Judgment.

[^145]: Allusion to the "lake of fire" (*stagnum ignis*) in Revelation 20:14–15.

[^146]: Heirmos of Ode 7, Tone 6, drawing upon the prayer of Azariah in Daniel 3:29–30.

[^147]: Heirmos of Ode 8, Tone 6, referencing Daniel 3:57ff. Note doxological formula: "unto all ages" (Greek: εἰς πάντας τοὺς αἰῶνας).

[^148]: Scriptural imagery of Gehenna: the river of fire (Daniel 7:10), outer darkness (Matthew 22:13), the undying worm and gnashing of teeth (Mark 9:48, Matthew 8:12).

[^149]: Heirmos of Ode 9, Tone 6, on the ineffable virginal incarnation of the Word. The Slavonic catchword at the foot of fol. 150r indicates the continuation: *ново-* ("renews natures...").

[^150]: Concluding lines of the Heirmos of Ode 9, Tone 6 (continued from Cohort 16, fol. 150r). The 1716 Kyiv imprint exhibits a typographical error (*ноко творитъ* for *новотворитъ*, "renews natures").

[^151]: The acrostic in the Slavonic text reads: *Даждь ми дѣво слезы покаянїа* ("Grant me, O Virgin, tears of repentance").

[^152]: Heirmos of Ode 1, Tone 6, drawing upon the crossing of the Red Sea in Exodus 14:21–31.

[^153]: Heirmos of Ode 3, Tone 6, drawing upon the Song of Hannah in 1 Kings (1 Samuel) 2:2.

[^154]: Allusion to Psalm 37:6 (LXX / Psalm 38:5): "My wounds stink and are corrupt because of my foolishness."

[^155]: Heirmos of Ode 4, Tone 6, drawing upon Habakkuk 3:2.

[^156]: Allusion to the deception of Eve by the serpent in Genesis 3:1–6.

[^157]: Heirmos of Ode 5, Tone 6, drawing upon Isaiah 26:9.

[^158]: Allusion to Psalm 12:5 (LXX / Psalm 13:4): "Lest mine enemy say: I have prevailed against him."

[^159]: Heirmos of Ode 6, Tone 6, drawing upon Jonah 2:2–7.

[^160]: Stavrotheotokion theme embedded within the Compline Sessional Hymn, lamenting the Passion before the Cross (John 19:25–27).

[^161]: Heirmos of Ode 7, Tone 6, drawing upon Daniel 3:49–50.

[^162]: Heirmos of Ode 8, Tone 6, drawing upon Daniel 3:57ff. Note doxological formula: "unto all ages" (Greek: εἰς πάντας τοὺς αἰῶνας).

[^163]: Heirmos of Ode 9, Tone 6 (the Great Saturday Heirmos, *Не рыдай Мене, Мати* / *Mē epodyrou mou, Mēter*).

[^164]: Allusion to the tree of the knowledge of good and evil and the fall of Adam and Eve in Genesis 3.

[^165]: Heirmos of Ode 1, Tone 6, drawing upon Exodus 15:1–2: "The Lord is my strength and song, and He is become my salvation."

[^166]: Heirmos of Ode 3, Tone 6, drawing upon 1 Kings (1 Samuel) 2:2.

[^167]: Heirmos of Ode 4, Tone 6, drawing upon Habakkuk 3:2.

[^168]: Dogmatic articulation of the hypostatic union: the eternal Word assumes human nature (*наше смѣшенїе*) from the Virgin's blood, existing twofold (divine and human) in one hypostasis (*въ единомъ составѣ* / ἐν μιᾷ ὑποστάσει).

[^169]: Heirmos of Ode 5, Tone 6, drawing upon Isaiah 26:9.

[^170]: Hymnographic locus addressing the Virgin as the "Impassable Gate" (*neprokhodimaya dveri* / ἡ ἀδιόδευτος πύλη), drawing upon the prophecy of the closed eastern gate in Ezekiel 44:1–3.

[^171]: Heirmos of Ode 6, Tone 6, drawing upon Jonah 2:2–7.

[^172]: Sessional Hymn of Friday Compline, celebrating the Incarnation as the reconciliation and elevation of earthly nature unto Heaven.

[^173]: Heirmos of Ode 7, Tone 6, drawing upon the penitential prayer of Azariah in Daniel 3:29–30 (LXX).

[^174]: Patristic formulation of Christ's two natures: the Word assumes human flesh possessing a rational soul (*odushévlennu neprelózhno* / ἔμψυχον νοεράν), renewing fallen nature.

[^175]: Heirmos of Ode 8, Tone 6, drawing upon Daniel 3:57ff. Note doxological formula: "unto all ages" (Greek: εἰς πάντας τοὺς αἰῶνας).

[^176]: Heirmos of Ode 9, Tone 6, evoking the Annunciation greeting of the Archangel Gabriel (Luke 1:28).

[^177]: Baroque rhymed four-line Polish-Ukrainian syllabic verse (*virshi*), composed in 13-syllable lines (*pariter conglutinata*) typical of Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra prints under Archimandrite Ioannykiv Halyatovsky and his successors, expressing the theological correlation between the Virgin standing beside Christ on earth and reigning beside Him in Heaven.

[^178]: Heirmos of Ode 1, Tone 7, drawing upon the Song of Moses in Exodus 15:1–4 ("Pharaoh's chariots and his host hath He cast into the sea; his chosen captains also are drowned in the Red Sea"). Note opening of Tone 7 at Saturday Compline.

[^179]: Heirmos of Ode 3, Tone 7, drawing upon Psalm 32:6 (LXX / Psalm 33:6: "By the word of the Lord were the heavens established") and Psalm 135:6 (LXX / Psalm 136:6: "To Him that established the earth above the waters").

[^180]: Heirmos of Ode 4, Tone 7, drawing upon Habakkuk 3:3 ("His glory covered the heavens, and the earth was full of His praise").

[^181]: Heirmos of Ode 5, Tone 7, drawing upon Genesis 1:1–3 (the primordial creation of light) and Isaiah 26:9.

[^390]: Luke 24:9–10.

[^392]: Slavonic *и҆з̾ нетлѣ́нїа* is an epigraphic typesetting anomaly in the 1716 Kyiv edition for *и҆з̾ тлѣ́нїа* (Greek: ἐκ φθορᾶς, "out of corruption"), as reflected in canonical Ruthenian and Byzantine witnesses.

[^393]: Hypakoe of the Resurrection, Tone 7.

[^394]: Luke 24:4–9.

[^395]: Ascribed in the Slavic Horologion to St. Basil the Great, though liturgically identical to the Prayer of the Hours composed in the Byzantine monastic tradition.

[^396]: 1 Tim. 1:17; Eph. 4:13.

[^397]: Paul, monk and founder of the Monastery of the Theotokos Evergetis near Constantinople (11th c.). The Slavonic rubric explains *Е҆ѵергети́дскїѧ* as *си́рѣчь, Ѡ҆би́тели Бл҃годѣ́телныхъ* ("that is, of the Monastery of the Benefactress / Benefactors").

[^398]: Classical Compline prayer of St. Paul of Evergetis (Ἄσπιλε, ἀμόλυντε, ἄφθορε...).

[^399]: Concluding petition and Trinitarian doxology of the Prayer of Paul of Evergetis at Compline.

[^400]: Antiochus of Palestine (monk of Mar Saba, 7th c.), author of the *Pandects of the Holy Scriptures*. His evening prayer *Καὶ δὸς ἡμῖν, Δέσποτα...* ("And grant unto us, O Master...") is a universal Horologion component of Compline.

[^401]: Ancient Marian prayer of intercession recited before sleep.

[^402]: Venerable Joannicius the Great of Mount Olympus (Bithynia, 9th c.). His famous prayer *Ἡ ἐλπίς μου ὁ Θεός...* was repeated by him after each verse of the Psalter.

[^403]: The penitential Troparia of Tone 6 (*Помилуй насъ, Господи...* and the Theotokion *Милосердія двери отверзи намъ*).

[^404]: The Litany of Fervent Supplication (*Ectene*) at Small Compline, including the petition for deliverance from plagues and natural disasters.

[^405]: Slavonic text reads *Бж҃е на́шъ* ("our God"), corresponding to the standard Byzantine liturgical formula.

[^406]: The Daily Dismissal (*Apodosis*) for Small Compline.

[^407]: The Great Lent and Holy Week rubrics prescribe the solemn prayer of absolution *Владыко Многомилостиве* ("O Master, great in mercy...").

[^408]: The Petitions for Peace (*Aitēseis* / Suffrages) recited at the conclusion of Small Compline.

[^409]: Russian imperial titulature formula as printed in Kyiv, 1716.

[^410]: Although the Russian Patriarchate was vacant after the death of Patriarch Adrian in 1700 (Stefan Yavorsky serving as locum tenens until Peter I established the Most Holy Synod in 1721), the 1716 Kyiv liturgical editions conservatively retained the traditional rubric for the Patriarch.

[^411]: The commemorative litany for the departed (*Litiya for the reposed*) appointed for weekdays.

[^412]: Slavonic *Правосла́вныѧ Цр҃ѧ̑ на́ша* ("our Orthodox Tsars").

[^413]: Dormition dedication of the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra Church.

[^414]: The Evening Prayers (*Prayers before Sleep*) form an integral Horologion sequence appended to Compline in Slavic editions.

[^415]: St. Macarius the Great of Egypt (4th c.). The first prayer of the Slavic Horologion before sleep.

[^416]: The second prayer before sleep by Antiochus the Monk (*Παντοκράτορ, Λόγε τοῦ Πατρός...*).

[^417]: Slavonic *сѣ́ма тлѝ во мнѣ̀ є҆́сть* ("the seed of corruption is in me").

[^418]: The third prayer before sleep, addressed to God the Holy Spirit, containing an exhaustive daily self-examination and confession of sins.

[^419]: The fourth prayer before sleep, attributed in the Greek Horologion to St. John Chrysostom.

[^420]: The fifth prayer before sleep, attributed to St. John Chrysostom or St. Basil.

[^421]: Peter the Studite (monk of the famous Studios Monastery in Constantinople).

[^422]: Allusion to Rom. 7:15–19 ("For what I would, that do I not; but what I hate, that do I").

[^423]: The seventh prayer before sleep, a traditional Byzantine supplication to the Theotokos (*Ἀγαθοῦ Βασιλέως ἀγαθὴ Μῆτερ...*).

[^424]: The eighth prayer before sleep, the daily prayer of devotion to one's Guardian Angel.

[^425]: *Kontakion of the Theotokos*: The celebrated prooimion of the Akathist Hymn (*Взбранной Воеводѣ*, Greek: *Τῇ ὑπερμάχῳ στρατηγῷ*), customarily appointed at the conclusion of Compline (*Повечерїе*) in the Byzantine and Ruthenian traditions.

[^426]: *Anthony and Theodosius of the Caves*: The insertion of the founders of the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra (*Антѡнїѧ и Ѳеодо́сїѧ Пече́рскихъ*) within the daily dismissal formula reflects the liturgical recension of the Kyiv Lavra printing house.

[^427]: *Prayer of Saint John of Damascus*: This nocturnal prayer of compunction (*Владыко Человѣколюбче, не ужели мнѣ одръ сей гробъ будетъ;*) is traditionally ascribed to Saint John of Damascus and forms a standard element of the Prayers before Sleep in the Ruthenian and Slavic Horologion.

[^428]: *Prostration unto the face of the earth*: Church Slavonic: *Поклоненїе до лица земли, по всемъ Правилѣ*. This rubric indicates a full metania / great prostration (*земной поклонъ*) to be made at the completion of the prescribed cell prayer rule.

[^429]: *Engraving of the Holy Trinity*: The woodcut presents the New Testament Trinity (*Сопрестолїе* / *Paternitas*), characteristic of 17th- and 18th-century Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra book engraving, showing God the Father with a tri-radiant or pontifical tiara and sceptre beside the Son, with the Holy Spirit hovering above.

[^430]: *Kept bound through the years*: Church Slavonic: *моѧ̑ лѣ́ты оу҆держа́ннаѧ... согрѣше́нїѧ*. Alluding to the apostolic authority to bind and loose sins (St. John 20:23), asking that accumulated sins retained over many years be loosed through the grace of the Holy Spirit.

[^431]: *Peter, Alexis, and Jonah, Russian wonderworkers*: The Three Moscow and All-Rus Hierarchs (*Пе́тре, А҆леѯі́е и҆ І҆ѡ́но, Рѡссі́йстїи чꙋдотво́рцы*), canonized in the 15th and 16th centuries, whose veneration was standard across East Slavic Sluzhebnik.

[^432]: *Roman and David, Russian wonderworkers*: The baptismal names of the holy Passion-bearers Boris and Gleb (*Рома́не и҆ Дѣ́де* [a misprint or recensional variant in the 1716 Kyiv edition for *Давы́де* / *Дв҃де*]), sons of Grand Prince Vladimir the Great.

[^433]: *Anthony and Theodosius of the Caves*: Church Slavonic: *А҆нтѡ́нїе и҆ Ѳеодѡ́сїе Пече́рстїи*. Re-invoked here specifically among the monastic fathers of the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra.

[^434]: *Agapitus*: Saint Agapitus the Unmercenary Physician of the Kyiv Caves (*Агапитъ Печерский*, d. 1095), invoked alongside the universal unmercenary physicians Cosmas and Damian, Cyrus and John, and Panteleimon.

[^435]: *Troparia of Compline*: These three troparia (*Просвѣти ѻчи мои*, *Заступникъ дши моєй*, *Преславную Хр̄тову Мтерь*) constitute the ancient nocturnal troparia appended to Compline and the midnight office in the Byzantine Horologion.

[^436]: *Enlighten mine eyes...*: Psalm 12:4–5 [Masoretic 13:3–4]: "Lighten mine eyes, lest I sleep the sleep of death; lest mine enemy say, I have prevailed against him."

[^437]: *Kiss thy cross...*: An ancient monastic and lay rubrical practice for retirement before sleep, blessing the bed on all four sides with the pectoral cross (*наперсный крестъ*) while reciting the Prayer to the Venerable Cross.

[^438]: *Prayer to the Precious and Life-Creating Cross of the Lord*: A traditional prayer based on Psalm 67:2–3 [Masoretic 68:1–2], combined with an apostrophe to the Cross (*Радуйся Пречестный и Животворящій Кресте Господень*).

[^439]: *Psalm 67:2–3 [Masoretic 68:1–2]*: "Let God arise, and let His enemies be scattered; let them also that hate Him flee before Him. As smoke is driven away, so drive them away: as wax melts before the fire, so let the wicked perish at the presence of God."

[^440]: *Colophon of the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra*: The concise printer's dedication and doxological closure (*Сотворшему Дѣвѣ Богоматери величїе Силному Богу...*) of the 1716 Kyiv edition of the Octoechos-Theotokarion (*Богородичникъ*).

[^441]: *Who hath done great things...*: An explicit quotation of the Magnificat (St. Luke 1:49): "For He that is mighty hath done to me great things; and holy is His name" (Church Slavonic: *ꙗ҆́кѡ сотвори́ ми вели́чїе Си́льный, и҆ ст҃о и҆́мѧ є҆гѡ̀*).

[^442]: *Printer's Mark of the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra*: The formal baroque ornamental tailpiece vignette characteristic of the Archimandrite and typography of the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra in the early 18th century, marking the formal end of the printed codex.

[^443]: *Manuscript Addition*: An early 18th-century cursive Cyrillic inscription written by a contemporary reader/monastic on the facing unnumbered recto leaf (Fol. [237r]) immediately following the printed colophon, providing practical ascetic guidance for the daily nocturnal examination of conscience (*испытанїе совѣсти*).

[^444]: *Settle accounts with thine own self*: Church Slavonic: *Положѝ сло́во съ сами́мъ тобо́ю*. A classical patristic idiom derived from the monastic tradition of daily self-examination (cf. Saint John Chrysostom, *Homily on the Statues*, and Saint Dorotheus of Gaza, *Discourses*), enjoining a methodical review of all deeds, words, and thoughts before sleep.
